(s_{v}(z)+s_{\theta})^{3}\log^{3}(np)\log^{3}(n)=o(nh^{2})
,
h^{2}s_{v}(z)=o(1)
,
r_{a}^{2}=O(\sqrt{n}h^{-1}\bar{r}_{B}^{2}+h^{2}),\varphi_{\max}(p)=O(1)
,
\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})=O(1)
, and
\|z\|_{2}\kappa_{2}^{-1}(\infty)=O(1)
. If the following limit

\displaystyle H(\tau_{1},\tau_{2};z):=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\tau_{1}\wedge\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}\tau_{2}}{4}v_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{1};z)\mathbb{E}[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau_{1})|X)f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau_{2})|X)XX^{\prime}]v_{0}(\tau_{2};z)

exists for all
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathcal{T}
, then

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\lambda,\gamma}(\cdot;z)-Q(\cdot;z)\right)\leadsto\mathbb{G}(\cdot\>;z)\hskip 9.24994pt{}\mathrm{in}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{T}),

where
\mathbb{G}(\cdot\>;z)
is a centered Gaussian process with covariance function
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\mapsto H(\tau_{1},\tau_{2};z)
.

Lemma 21
.

Recall the setup of Corollary
3
. With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathcal{T}}\left|\widehat{H}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2};z)-H(\tau_{1},\tau_{2};z)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{7}\left(\frac{\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\vee 1\right)\Big(\bar{r}_{B}+\sqrt{n}\sqrt{s_{v}(z)}h^{-1}\bar{r}_{B}^{2}+\sqrt{s_{v}(z)}h^{2}\Big),

where
C_{7}>0
is a constant depending on
c_{0},\bar{f},L_{f},L_{\theta},C_{Q},\kappa_{2}(\infty),\varphi_{\max}(p)
only, and

\displaystyle\widehat{H}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2};z)=\hat{v}_{\gamma}^{\prime}(\tau_{1};z)\left(\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}(\tau_{1})\hat{f}_{i}(\tau_{2})X_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}\right)\hat{v}_{\gamma}(\tau_{2};z).

G.6
Auxiliary results

Lemma 22
.

Let
\alpha\geq 0,\beta>0,\gamma\geq 1
. Let
v\in C^{p}_{2}\left(T,\alpha\right)
and
\tilde{v}:=t(v_{T},0)^{\prime}+(0,v_{T^{c}})^{\prime}
with
|t|\geq\beta
. Then,
\tilde{v}\in C^{p}_{2}\left(T,\alpha/\beta\right)
. Moreover, if
v\in C^{p}_{2}\left(T,\alpha\right)
, then
v\in C^{p}_{2}\left(T,\alpha\gamma\right)
.

Lemma 23
.

Let
0\leq r_{a}\leq 1/4
and
M\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
,
r\geq 1
. Recall that
T_{v}(\tau;z)=\mathrm{support}\big(\tilde{v}(\tau;z)\big)
. The following holds true:

(i)

v_{0}(\tau;z)\in C^{p}_{2}\left(T_{v}(\tau;z),\frac{r_{a}}{1-r_{a}}\right)
;

(ii)

\left\|M^{\prime}\big(v_{0}(\tau;z)-\tilde{v}(\tau;z)\big)\right\|_{2}\leq\sup_{u\in C^{p}_{2}\big(T_{v}(\tau;z),1\big)\cap B_{2}^{p}\big(0,8r_{a}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\big)}\|M^{\prime}u\|_{2}
.

Lemma 24

(Restricted cone and cross-polytope property)
.

For
c_{0}>1
set
\bar{c}=\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}
. Suppose that Assumption
10
holds,

\displaystyle\gamma c_{0}^{-1}\geq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)X_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau;z)+nz\right\|_{\infty},

(39)

and

\displaystyle\mu c_{0}^{-1}\geq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{u\in C^{p}_{2}(T_{v}(\tau;z),1)\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,r_{a})}\left|\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)(X_{i}^{\prime}u)^{2}\right|.

(40)

Then, for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,

\displaystyle\bar{c}\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau;z)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|+\frac{\bar{c}\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\geq\sum_{k\in T_{v}^{c}(\tau;z)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|,

(41)

and

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{\gamma}(\tau;z)-\tilde{v}(\tau;z)\in C^{p}_{1}\big(T_{v}(\tau;z),2\bar{c}\big)\cup B^{p}_{1}\left(0,\frac{2\bar{c}\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\right).

(42)

Remark 14
.

If
r_{a}=0
, i.e.
\tilde{v}(\tau,z)=v_{0}(\tau;z)
, we can take
\mu=0
and the lemma reduces to the classical restricted cone property
(
Bickel et al., 2009
;
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
;
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2013
, e.g.)
. Note that inequality (
41
) implies that
\hat{v}_{\gamma}(\tau;z)-\tilde{v}(\tau;z)
lies in a so-called star-shaped set. However, since we are unaware of useful metric entropy bounds for star-shaped sets, we will use relation (
42
).

Lemma 25

(Lipschitz continuity of
\tau\mapsto v_{0}(\tau;z)
)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compact subset of
(0,1)
. Suppose that Assumptions
4
,
5
,
9
, and
10
hold. There exists an absolute constant
C_{v}\geq 1
such that for all
s,\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,

\displaystyle\left\|v_{0}(s;z)-v_{0}(\tau;z)\right\|_{2}\leq C_{v}\bar{f}L_{f}L_{\theta}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\frac{\varphi_{\max}(p)}{\kappa_{2}(\infty)}\|z\|_{2}|s-\tau|.

Lemma 26

(Maxima of sum of block multi-convex functions)
.

Let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{N}:\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{X}_{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be block multi-convex functions. Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X}_{j}),1\leq j\leq K}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})=\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j},1\leq j\leq K}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K}).

Moreover, the identity remains true if
\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}
is replaced by
\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\right|
.

Remark 15
.

This is a generalization of Lemma
12
.

Lemma 27
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compact subset of
(0,1)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\vartheta_{k}\in[0,\infty]
,
q_{k}\geq 1
,
J_{k}(\tau)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,
s_{k}=\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{card}(J_{k}(\tau))
,
p\geq s_{k}+2
, and
k\in\{1,2\}
. Let
\{(X_{i},Y_{i},\xi_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. random vectors. Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
3
hold and the
\xi_{i}
’s are i.i.d. standard Gaussian random variables. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\big\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y,\xi)=\xi\left(\tau-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2}),

\displaystyle u_{k}\in C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>k\in\{1,2\},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}.

With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\sqrt{s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}(s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+\log(1/\delta))},

where
\pi_{n,1/3}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z^{3}/n
with
z\geq 0
.

Lemma 28
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compact subset of
(0,1)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\vartheta_{k}\in[0,\infty]
,
q_{k}\geq 1
,
J_{k}(\tau)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,
s_{k}=\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{card}(J_{k}(\tau))
,
p\geq s_{k}+2
, and
k\in\{1,2\}
. Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
5
, and
9
hold. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\big\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X)=f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2}),

\displaystyle\>u_{k}\in C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>k\in\{1,2\},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}.

The following holds true:

(i)

With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}^{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta}))\psi_{n}\big(t_{s_{\theta},s_{1},s_{2},n,\delta}\big),

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(nL_{f}L_{\theta}/\delta)
and
\psi_{n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(1+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{z}+n^{-3/2}z^{3/2}\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

(ii)

Statement (i) holds also for the function class
|\mathcal{G}|:=\left\{h:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:\>\exists g\in\mathcal{G}:h=|g|\right\}
.

(iii)

Statements (i) and (ii) hold with
\vartheta_{1}=1
also for the function class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle=\big\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X)=f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)(X^{\prime}v)(X^{\prime}u),

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\|v\|_{0}\leq s,\>\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\>u\in C^{p}_{q}(J_{2}(\tau),\vartheta_{2})\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}.

Lemma 29
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compact subset of
(0,1)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary,
\vartheta\in[0,\infty]
,
q\geq 1
,
J(\tau)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,
s=\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{card}(J(\tau))
, and
p\geq s+2
. Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
5
, and
9
hold. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\left(\tau-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v,\right.

\displaystyle\left.v\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

The following holds true:

(i)

With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\lesssim(2+\vartheta)\bar{f}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\psi_{n}\big(t_{s,s_{\theta},n,\delta}\big),

where
t_{s,s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(nL_{f}L_{\theta}/\delta)
and
\psi_{n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(1+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{z}+n^{-3/2}z^{3/2}\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

(ii)

Statement (i) holds with
\vartheta=1
also for the function class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\left(\tau-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v,\right.

\displaystyle\left.v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\|v\|_{0}\leq s,\>\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

Lemma 30
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compact subset of
(0,1)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\vartheta_{k}\in[0,\infty]
,
q_{k}\geq 1
,
J_{k}(\tau)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,
s_{k}=\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{card}(J_{k}(\tau))
,
p\geq s_{k}+2
, and
k\in\{1,2\}
. Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
5
, and
9
hold. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\left(\tau-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2}),\right.

\displaystyle\left.u_{k}\in C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>k\in\{1,2\},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

The following holds true:

(i)

With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}^{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\psi_{n}\big(t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},n,\delta}\big),

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(nL_{f}L_{\theta}/\delta)
and
\psi_{n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(1+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{z}+n^{-3/2}z^{2}\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

(ii)

Statement (i) holds with
\vartheta_{1}=1
also for the function class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\left(\tau-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)(X^{\prime}v)(X^{\prime}u),\right.

\displaystyle\left.v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\|v\|_{0}\leq s,\>\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\>u\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

Lemma 31
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a compact subset of
(0,1)
. Let
r_{0}>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary,
\vartheta\in[0,\infty]
,
q\geq 1
,
J(\tau)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,
s=\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{card}(J(\tau))
, and
p\geq s+2
. Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
5
and
9
hold. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\left(\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\big\}-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v,\>\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\|\theta\|_{0}\leq n,\>\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0},\>v\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

The following holds true:

(i)

With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\forall g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}:\>|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{v,\theta,\tau})|

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta)\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\|\theta\|_{0}+s_{\theta})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\Big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n}\big(\|\theta\|_{0}\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(t_{s,\|\theta\|_{0},s_{\theta},n,\delta})\Big).

where
t_{s,k,s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+k\log(ep/k)+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(L_{f}L_{\theta}n/\delta)
and
\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(\sqrt{r_{0}}+n^{-1/2}(\log n)\sqrt{z}+n^{-1}(\log n)^{3/2}z\big)
for
z\geq 0
;

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{G}(m)=\left\{g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}:\|\theta\|_{0}\leq m\right\}
. With probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\forall m\leq n\wedge p:\>\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}(m)}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta)\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(m+s_{\theta})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\Big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n}\big(m\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(t_{s,m,s_{\theta},n,\delta})\Big),

where
t_{s,m,s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+m\log(ep/m)+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(L_{f}L_{\theta}n/\delta)
and
\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(\sqrt{r_{0}}+n^{-1/2}(\log n)\sqrt{z}+n^{-1}(\log n)^{3/2}z\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

(iii)

Statements (i) and (ii) hold also for the function class
|\mathcal{G}|:=\left\{h:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:\>\exists g\in\mathcal{G}:h=|g|\right\}
.

(iv)

Statements (i), (ii), and (iii) hold with
\vartheta=1
also for the function class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\left(\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\big\}-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v,\>\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\|\theta\|_{0}\leq n,\>\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0},\>\|v\|_{0}\leq s,\>\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

Appendix H
Results from empirical process theory

H.1
Maximal and deviation inequalities

In this section we collect maximal inequalities that we use throughout the proofs.

Definition 11

(Exponential Orlicz-norms (
\psi_{\alpha}
-norms))
.

For
\alpha>0
set
\psi_{\alpha}(x)=\exp(x^{\alpha})-1
. We define the exponential Orlicz-norm of a real-valued random variable
\xi
on
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
as

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}:=\inf\left\{\lambda>0:\mathbb{E}\psi_{\alpha}(|\xi|/\lambda)\leq 1\right\},

and the
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm of a real-valued
Q
-integrable function
f
on
(S,\mathcal{S})
as

\displaystyle\|f\|_{Q,\psi_{\alpha}}:=\inf\left\{\lambda>0:\int\psi_{\alpha}(|f|/\lambda)dQ\leq 1\right\}.

Remark 16
.

For
\alpha\in(0,1]
the map
t\mapsto\psi_{\alpha}(t)
is not convex; hence,
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{Q,\psi_{\alpha}}
are only quasinorms.

Theorem 8

(
Giessing, 2022
,
Giessing, 2022
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset L_{1}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
and
\rho
be a pseudo-metric on
\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{F}
. For
\delta>0
define
\mathcal{F}_{\delta}=\{f-g:f,g\in\mathcal{F},\rho(f,g)\leq\delta\}
. Suppose that for
\alpha>0
there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\|(f-Pf)-(g-Pg)\|_{P,\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K\rho(f,g).

(43)

The following holds true:

(i)

If
\alpha\in(0,1]
, then for all events
A\in\mathcal{A}
with
\mathbb{P}\{A\}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}\mid A\right]\leq C_{\alpha}K\left[\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)}{\mathbb{P}\{A\}}\right)}d\varepsilon+n^{-1/2}\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\log\left(\frac{N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)}{\mathbb{P}\{A\}}\right)\right)^{1/\alpha}d\varepsilon\right],

where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant depending on
\alpha
only.

(ii)

If
\alpha\in(1,2]
, then for all events
A\in\mathcal{A}
with
\mathbb{P}\{A\}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}\mid A\right]\leq C_{\alpha}K\left[\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)}{\mathbb{P}\{A\}}\right)}d\varepsilon+n^{-1/2+1/\beta}\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\log\left(\frac{N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)}{\mathbb{P}\{A\}}\right)\right)^{1/\alpha}d\varepsilon\right],

where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant depending on
\alpha
only and
1/\alpha+1/\beta=1
.

Remark 17
.

The Lipschitz-type condition (
43
) on the individual increments can be substituted by a Lipschitz condition on the uncentered individual increments, i.e. cases (i) and (ii) continue to hold true (with a larger constant) if there exist
K>0
such that for all
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
,
\|f-g\|_{P,\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K\rho(f,g)
. We establish this claim as a side result in the proof of Theorem
8
.

Remark 18
.

If
\mathcal{F}=\{f_{t}:t\in T\}
and
\|(f_{s}-Pf_{s})-(f_{t}-Pf_{t})\|_{P,\psi_{\alpha}}\leq Kd(s,t)
for all
f_{s},f_{t}\in\mathcal{F}
and a pseudo-metric
d
on
T\times T
, then we can replace
N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)
by
N(\varepsilon,T,d)
.

Corollary 4

(
Giessing, 2022
,
Giessing, 2022
)
.

Recall the setup of Theorem
8
. The following holds true:

(i)

If
\alpha\in(0,1]
, then, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}K\left(\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)}d\varepsilon+n^{-1/2}\int_{0}^{\delta}\big(\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)\big)^{1/\alpha}d\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+C_{\alpha}K\delta\left(\sqrt{t}+n^{-1/2}t^{1/\alpha}\right),

where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant depending on
\alpha
only.

(ii)

If
\alpha\in(1,2]
, then, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}K\left(\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)}d\varepsilon+n^{-1/2+1/\beta}\int_{0}^{\delta}\big(\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},\rho)\big)^{1/\alpha}d\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+C_{\alpha}K\delta\left(\sqrt{t}+n^{-1/2+1/\beta}t^{1/\alpha}\right),

where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant depending on
\alpha
only and
1/\alpha+1/\beta=1
.

Lemma 32

(Theorem 5.2,
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
,
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
with envelope
F\in L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
and
0\in\mathcal{F}
. Let
\sigma^{2}>0
be any positive constant such that
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}Pf^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}\leq\|F\|_{P,2}^{2}
. Set
\eta=\sigma/\|F\|_{P,2}
. Define
M=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}F(X_{i})
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim J(\eta,\mathcal{F})\|F\|_{P,2}+\frac{\|M\|_{2}J^{2}(\eta,\mathcal{F})}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{n}},

where

\displaystyle J(\eta,\mathcal{F})=\int_{0}^{\eta}\sup_{Q}\sqrt{\log N\big(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\big)}d\varepsilon,

and the supremum is taken over all finitely discrete probability measures
Q
.

Lemma 33

(Corollary 5.1,
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
,
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
be a VC type class of functions with envelope
F\in L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
and
0\in\mathcal{F}
. Let
\sigma^{2}>0
be any positive constant such that
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}Pf^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}\leq\|F\|_{P,2}^{2}
. Set
\eta=\sigma/\|F\|_{P,2}
. Define
M=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}F(X_{i})
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim\sqrt{V\sigma^{2}\log\left(\frac{A\|F\|_{P,2}}{\sigma}\right)}+\frac{V\|M\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\log\left(\frac{A\|F\|_{P,2}}{\sigma}\right).

Lemma 34

(Theorem 4,
Adamczak, 2008
,
Adamczak, 2008
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
with envelope
F\in L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
and
0\in\mathcal{F}
. Let
\sigma^{2}>0
be any positive constant such that
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}Pf^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}\leq\|F\|_{P,2}^{2}
. Define
M=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}F(X_{i})
. For all
\alpha\in(0,1]
and
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}+\sigma\sqrt{t}+\|M\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}n^{-1/2}t^{1/\alpha}.

Lemma 35

(Lemma 7.1,
Kley et al., 2016
,
Kley et al., 2016
)
.

Let
\{X_{t}:t\in T\}
be a separable stochastic process with
\|X_{s}-X_{t}\|_{P,\Psi}\leq Cd(s,t)
(
\|\cdot\|_{P,\Psi}
denotes the
\Psi
-Orlicz with respect to measure
P
) for all
s,t
satisfying
d(s,t)\geq\bar{\eta}/2\geq 0
. Denote by
D(\epsilon,T,d)
the packing number of the metric space
(T,d)
. Then, for any
\delta>0
,
\eta\geq\bar{\eta}
, there exists a random variable
S_{1}
and a constant
K<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{d(s,t)\leq\delta}|X_{s}-X_{t}|\leq S_{1}+2\sup_{d(s,t)\leq\bar{\eta},t\in\widetilde{T}}|X_{s}-X_{t}|,

and

\displaystyle\|S_{1}\|_{P,\Psi}\leq K\left[\int_{\bar{\eta}/2}^{\eta}\Psi^{-1}\big(D(\epsilon,T,d)\big)d\epsilon+(\delta+2\bar{\eta})\Psi^{-1}\big(D^{2}(\eta,T,d)\big)\right],

where the set
\widetilde{T}
contains at most
D(\bar{\eta},T,d)
points. In particular, by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{|S_{1}|>x\right\}\leq\left(\Psi\left(x\left[8K\left(\int_{\bar{\eta}/2}^{\eta}\Psi^{-1}\big(D(\epsilon,T,d)\big)d\epsilon+(\delta+2\bar{\eta})\Psi^{-1}\big(D^{2}(\eta,T,d)\big)\right)\right]^{-1}\right)\right)^{-1}.

H.2
Auxiliary results

In this section we collect technical auxiliary results that we use throughout the proofs.

Corollary 5
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\subset L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
a finite collection of functions with
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})<\infty
. Let
\sigma^{2}>0
be a positive constant such that
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}Pg^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}
. Let
\rho
be a semi-metric on
\mathcal{G}^{2}\times\mathcal{G}^{2}
with
\mathcal{G}^{2}=\{g^{2}:g\in\mathcal{G}\}
and
\delta>0
be a positive constant such that
\sup_{g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{G}^{2}}\rho(g_{1},g_{2})\leq\delta
. Let
\mathcal{H}\subset L_{1}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
be a collection of VC subgraph functions with VC-index
V(\mathcal{H})
and absolute values bounded by one. Define
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{G}\mathcal{H}=\{x\mapsto g(x)h(x):g\in\mathcal{G},\>h\in\mathcal{H}\}
. If there exists a constant
K>0
such that for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
and all
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{G}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|(g_{1}-Pg_{1})-(g_{2}-Pg_{2})\|_{P,\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K\rho(g_{1},g_{2}),

(44)

then, there exist constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
(depending on
\alpha
) such that for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-ce^{-c^{\prime}t}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim\left(\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})+\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})}+\sqrt{t}\right)\left(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}\big(\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})\big)}+\sqrt{K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(t)}\right),

where
\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z^{1/\alpha}/n
for
z\geq 0
.

Remark 19
.

A similar result holds for
\alpha\in(1,2]
with explicit constants
c=3e,c^{\prime}=1
; however, we do not need such a result in this paper.

Remark 20
.

Note that the upper bound does not depend on the envelope of
\mathcal{F}
. The bound is therefore better by at least a
\log n
-factor than if we had used a combination of Lemma
34
and Lemma
33
. Typically, the quantities
K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}\big(\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})\big)
and
K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(t)
will be negligible compared to
\sigma^{2}
.

Remark 21
.

We use this result to derive sharp bounds on the gradient of the check loss (i.e. the loss function of the quantile regression program). To establish the connection between this result and quantile regression note that the (sub)gradient of the check loss
\{\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}X^{\prime}v
can be written as the product of two functions,
h(X,Y)=\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}=\tau-\mathbf{1}\{F_{Y|X}(Y|X)\leq\tau\}
and
g(X)=X^{\prime}v
. The function
h
is bounded in absolute value by one and belongs to a class of VC subgraph functions with VC-index at most 3 (the difference of two classes of VC-subgraph functions with VC-index at most 2) and
g
is indexed by
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. In our specific applications, we will be able to argue that
v\in\mathcal{M}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})<\infty
.

Lemma 36

(Orlicz-norm of products of random variables)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{K}\in\mathbb{R}
be random variables with finite
\psi_{\alpha}
-Orlicz-norm. Then, for
K\geq 1
,
\|\prod_{k=1}^{K}X_{k}\|_{\psi_{\alpha/K}}\leq\prod_{k=1}^{K}\|X_{k}\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
.

Lemma 37
.

Let
\phi,\varphi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
be increasing functions and
\xi
be a random variable on
L_{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
. If for all events
A\in\mathcal{A}
with
\mathbb{P}\{A\}>0
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\xi\mid A\right]\leq\phi\left(\varphi^{-1}\left(1/\mathbb{P}\{A\}\right)\right)
, then for all
u>0
,
\mathbb{P}\big\{\xi>\phi(u)\big\}\leq\big(\varphi(u)\big)^{-1}
.

Lemma 38

(Useful generalization of Lemma 1,
Panchenko, 2003
,
Panchenko, 2003
)
.

Let
X,Y
be random variables such that
\mathbb{E}[F(X)]\leq\mathbb{E}[F(Y)]
for every convex and increasing function
F
. Further, let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a concave and strictly increasing function and
\alpha\in(0,\infty)
. If for some constants
c_{1}>1
,
c_{2}>0
, and for all
t\geq 0
,
\mathbb{P}\left\{Y\geq\varphi\big(t^{1/\alpha}\big)\right\}\leq c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t}
, then there exist constants
c_{3}>1
,
c_{4}>0
(depending only on
c_{1},c_{2},\alpha
) such that, for all
t\geq 0
,
\mathbb{P}\left\{X\geq\varphi\big(t^{1/\alpha}\big)\right\}\leq c_{3}e^{-c_{4}t}
.

Remark 22
.

It is possible to find explicit expressions for the constants
c_{3},c_{4}
in terms of
c_{1},c_{2},\alpha
. For our purposes the constants are irrelevant, only the nature of the inherited tail behavior is important. However, for completeness, we record that for
\alpha\in[1,\infty)
the result holds with
c_{3}=c_{1}e
and
c_{4}=c_{2}
. The main use of this lemma is to deduce tail bounds for empirical processes based on tail bounds on the corresponding symmetrized empirical process.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{H}\subset L_{2}(S,\mathcal{S},P)
. Further,
\varphi_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
i\leq n
, be a contraction with
\varphi_{i}(0)=0
and
F:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
be convex and increasing. Let
\{g_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard Gaussian random variables independent of
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\frac{1}{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\varphi_{i}\big(f(X_{i})\big)h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(2\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}f(X_{i})h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right].

Remark 23
.

It would be desirable to derive an analogous result with Rademacher instead of standard Gaussian random variables. For bounded function classes such a result can be easily obtained via Theorem 2 in
Maurer, 2016
; for unbounded function classes it is unclear whether such a result holds true.

Appendix I
Proofs of the results in the supplementary materials

I.1
Proofs of Section
F.3

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Ansatz.

For
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
and
r>0
(to be specified below) define
B^{p}(0,r):=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|v\|_{2}\leq r\}
and recall that
C^{p}(J,\vartheta)=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\theta_{J^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\vartheta\|\theta_{J}\|_{1}\right\}
. Suppose that, with high probability,

(a)

\hat{\theta}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}\big(T_{\theta}(\tau),\bar{c}\big)
for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
; and

(b)

for some absolute constant
c>0
, the regularized and centered objective function is strictly positive when evaluated at points
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathcal{T}
satisfying
\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}(T_{\theta}(\tau),\bar{c})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,r/(2c))=:K(r/(2c),\tau)
, i.e.

\displaystyle\inf_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\inf_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{\tau}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}\big(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)+\lambda\big(\|\theta\|_{1}-\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}\big)\right)>0.

Since the regularized and centered objective function is convex in
\theta
and negative at
\widehat{\theta}(\tau)
for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
, it follows that
\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\|\widehat{\theta}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\lesssim r
. Thus, to establish the claim of the theorem, we only need to prove that statements (a) and (b) hold with high probability.

Verification of high probability statements.
By assumption, eq. (
25
) holds true. Thus, by Lemma
8
, statement (a) holds for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
with probability one. We are left with establishing statement (b). Suppose that
r>0
is such that Assumption
7
holds with
(\vartheta,\varrho)=(\bar{c},r)
. Then,
q_{r}(\bar{c})\gtrsim 1
, i.e. there exists an absolute constant
c\geq 1
such that
c\cdot q_{r}(\bar{c})\geq 1
. Set

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}:=\big\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=\rho_{\tau}\big(Y-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)-\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta),\>\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T},\>\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)\big\}.

Compute

\displaystyle\inf_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\inf_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{\tau}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}\big(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)+\frac{\lambda}{n}\big(\|\theta\|_{1}-\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}\big)\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim-\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},Y_{i})-\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\frac{\lambda}{n}\big(\|\theta\|_{1}-\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\gtrsim-\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},Y_{i})\right]-n^{-1/2}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}-\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\frac{\lambda}{n}\big(\|\theta\|_{1}-\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}\big).

(45)

We bound the expressions on the far right hand side in above display. By Lemma
10
,

\displaystyle-\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},Y_{i})\right]=\inf_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-g(X_{i},Y_{i})\right]\gtrsim\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})r^{2}.

(46)

By Lemma
13
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\lesssim 2(2+\bar{c})\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})r\sqrt{\frac{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\delta)+\log(1+L_{\theta}/r)}{n}}.

(47)

By the reverse triangle inequality and Lemma
8
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\frac{\lambda}{n}\big(\|\theta\|_{1}-\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}\big).

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}}\big(|\theta_{k}|-|\theta_{0}(\tau)|\big)+\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}^{c}}|\theta_{k}|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}}|\theta_{k}-\theta_{0}(\tau)|+\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}^{c}}|\theta_{k}|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq(1+\bar{c})\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r/(2c),\tau)}\frac{\lambda}{n}\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}}|\theta_{k}-\theta_{0}(\tau)|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim(1+\bar{c})\frac{\lambda}{n}s_{\theta}^{1/2}r.

(48)

Combine eq. (
46
), (
47
), and (
I.1
), use Assumption
6
, and observe that, there exist absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
such that with probability at least
1-\delta
, the expression in eq. (
I.1
) can be lower bounded (up to a multiplicative constant) by

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})r^{2}

\displaystyle-c_{1}2(2+\bar{c})\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})r\sqrt{\frac{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1+L_{\theta}/r)+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle-c_{2}(1+\bar{c})\frac{\lambda}{n}s_{\theta}^{1/2}r

\displaystyle>0,

whenever

\displaystyle r\geq c_{3}\left(C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log n+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\bigvee\frac{\bar{c}}{\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})}\frac{\lambda\sqrt{s_{\theta}}}{n}\right),

where
C_{1}:=\frac{\bar{c}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}}{\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})}
(recall that
L_{\theta}\geq 1
) and
\bar{c}>1
is given in Lemma
8
. By assumption,
(\bar{c},r)
are such that the
(\vartheta,\varrho)
-restricted identifiability Assumption
7
holds. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1
.

The idea is to derive an upper bound on the gradient of the quantile loss function. Any
\lambda>0
greater than this upper bound, will satisfy eq. (
25
). By Lemma
15
(ii), with probability at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\|v\|_{0}=1,\|v\|_{2}\leq 1}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}v\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{n\log(p/\delta)}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}(\log(p/\delta))},

where
\pi_{n,1}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z/n
for
z\geq 0
and
\sigma_{\max}=\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(1)
. Thus, by Assumption
6
, there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{n\log(p/\delta)}.

Therefore, any
\lambda\geq C_{1}c_{0}\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{n\log(p/\delta)}
satisfies eq. (
25
) with probability at least
1-\delta
. Plug this lower bound on
\lambda>0
into the rate
r>0
from Theorem
3
and simplify the expression using Assumption
6
.
∎

I.2
Proofs of Section
F.4

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Proof of statement (i).
Recall from Lemma 9 in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
that
\hat{s}\leq n\wedge p
. From the complementary slackness characterizations (C.3) in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
we obtain the following inequality:

\displaystyle\lambda\hat{s}

\displaystyle=\lambda\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{sign}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\mathrm{sign}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{sign}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\hat{a}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq n\sqrt{\hat{s}}\left(\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1,\>\|u\|_{0}\leq\hat{s}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}^{\prime}u)^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq n\sqrt{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(\hat{s})\hat{s}}.

This implies that for all
\lambda>0
,

\displaystyle\lambda\leq n\sqrt{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(\hat{s})/\hat{s}}.

Combine above inequality with the lower bound
\lambda\geq n\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(m)/m}
to obtain

\displaystyle\hat{s}\leq\frac{m}{2}\frac{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(\hat{s})}{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(m)}.

We now proceed by contradiction as in the proof of Lemma 6 in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
: Suppose that
\hat{s}>m
. Then there exists
\ell>1
such that
\hat{s}=\ell m
. Therefore, by Lemma 13 in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
,

\displaystyle\hat{s}\leq\frac{m}{2}\frac{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(\ell m)}{\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(m)}\leq\frac{m}{2}\lceil\ell\rceil<\ell m=\hat{s}.

Thus,
\hat{s}\leq m
. This concludes the proof of statement (i).

Proof of statement (ii).

The following proof is modeled after the proof of Lemma 7 in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
with necessary modifications to accommodate our setup.

First, since
s\mapsto\varphi_{\max}(s)\vee\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(s)
is non-decreasing, we have

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=C_{\lambda}\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}\left(\frac{n}{\log(np/\delta)}\right)\vee\hat{\varphi}_{\max}\left(\frac{n}{\log(np/\delta)}\right)}\sqrt{n\log(np/\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq C_{\lambda}\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(1)\vee\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(1)}\sqrt{n\log(p/\delta)}.

Thus, arguing as in the proof of Corollary
1
, we have for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}(T_{\theta}(\tau),\bar{c}),

and, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\lesssim\widetilde{C}_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\theta}\log(np/\delta)}{n}},

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{1}:=\frac{\bar{c}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}}{\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})}\vee\frac{C_{\lambda}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}\big(n/\log(np/\delta)\big)}{\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})}\vee\frac{C_{\lambda}\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}^{1/2}\big(n/\log(np/\delta)\big)}{\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})}\vee 1.

(49)

In the following, to simplify the notation, we write
\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)=\mathrm{support}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\big)
,
\mathbf{X}=[X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}]
, and
\hat{s}(\tau)=\|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\|_{0}
. Also, denote by
\hat{a}(\tau)=(\hat{a}_{1}(\tau),\ldots,\hat{a}_{n}(\tau))
, the (vector of) dual optimal rank scores which solve the dual program (C.2) in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
. From the complementary slackness characterizations (C.3) and identity (C.4) in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
we obtain the following inequality:

\displaystyle\lambda\sqrt{\hat{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}-\hat{a}_{i}(\tau)\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}+\mathbf{III}.

(50)

Bound on
\mathbf{I}
.
Observe that
\hat{a}_{i}(\tau)\neq\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}
only if
Y_{i}=X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)
. By Lemma 9 in
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
the penalized quantile regression fit can only interpolate at most
\hat{s}(\tau)\leq\hat{s}
points almost surely uniformly over
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
. This implies that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}-\hat{a}_{i}(\tau)\big|\leq\hat{s}
. Therefore, by Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
17
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}-\hat{a}_{i}(\tau)\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sup_{\mathrm{card}(I)\leq\hat{s}}\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1,\|u\|_{0}\leq\hat{s}}\left|\sum_{i\in I}X_{i}^{\prime}u\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sup_{\mathrm{card}(I)\leq\hat{s}}\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1,\|u\|_{0}\leq\hat{s}}\hat{s}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{s}}\sum_{i\in I}(X_{i}^{\prime}u)^{2}-E[(X_{i}^{\prime}u)^{2}]\right)^{1/2}+\hat{s}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\hat{s}\sqrt{\log(epn/\hat{s})}+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\hat{s}^{3/4}\big(\log(1/\delta)\big)^{1/4}+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\hat{s}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+\hat{s}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s}),

(51)

where we have used that
\phi_{\max}(\hat{s})\leq\varphi_{\max}(\hat{s})
.

Bound on
\mathbf{II}
.
Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=\left(1\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\big\}-1\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v,\right.

\displaystyle\left.v,\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\>\|v\|_{0}\leq n,\>\|\theta\|_{0}\leq n,\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\},

and, for
1\leq s\leq n\wedge p
and
r=\widetilde{C}_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s_{\theta}\log(np/\delta)}{n}}
with
\widetilde{C}_{1}\geq 1
defined in eq. (
49
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(s)=\big\{g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}:\|v\|_{0}\leq s,\|\theta\|_{0}\leq s\big\}\bigcap\big\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\>\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}(T_{\theta}(\tau),\bar{c})\cap B^{p}(0,r),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}.

By the triangle inequality and Corollary
1
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}(\hat{s})}+n\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}(\hat{s})}|Pg|.

(52)

By Lemma
16
(ii), with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}(\hat{s})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\hat{s}\log(ep/\hat{s})+\log(n/\delta)}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}\big(\hat{s}\log(ep/\hat{s})+\log(n/\delta)\big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\hat{s}\log(ep/\hat{s})+\log(n/\delta)},

(53)

where the second inequality holds since
\hat{s}\leq n/\log(np/\delta)
by choice of
\lambda>0
and statement (i).

By Assumption
5
, for all
g\in\mathcal{G}(\hat{s})
,

\displaystyle nPg

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}\left[X^{\prime}v\int_{X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)}^{X^{\prime}\theta}\big(f_{Y|X}(z|X)-f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\big)dz\right]+v^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\right]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\leq n\frac{L_{f}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{\prime}v|\big(X^{\prime}(\theta_{0}(\tau)-\theta)\big)^{2}\right]+nv^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\right]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big).

(54)

Since
\tau\mapsto f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)
is continuous on the compact set
\mathcal{T}
, it is necessarily bounded. Thus, by Lemmas
11
and
12
,

\displaystyle nv^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\right]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\lesssim(2+\bar{c})n\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})r.

(55)

By Hölder’s inequality, Assumption
1
, and Lemmas
11
and
12
,

\displaystyle n\frac{L_{f}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{\prime}v|\big(X^{\prime}(\theta_{0}(\tau)-\theta)\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\frac{L_{f}}{2}\big(\mathbb{E}[|X^{\prime}v|^{3}]\big)^{1/3}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\big(X^{\prime}(\theta_{0}(\tau)-\theta)\big)^{3}\big]\Big)^{2/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\bar{c})^{2}nL_{f}\big(\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})+\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\big)\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})r^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\bar{c})^{2}nL_{f}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})r^{2}.

(56)

Combine eq. (
52
)–(
I.2
) and conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\hat{s}\log(ep/\hat{s})+\log(n/\delta)}+(2+\bar{c})^{2}nL_{f}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})r^{2}+(2+\bar{c})n\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})r.\end{split}

(57)

Bound on
\mathbf{III}
.
Since
\lambda>0
satisfies eq. (
25
), Hölder’s inequality yields, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{2}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sqrt{\hat{s}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big)X_{i,\widehat{T}_{\theta}(\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq c_{0}^{-1}\lambda\sqrt{\hat{s}}.\end{split}

(58)

Conclusion.
Combine eq. (
I.2
), (
I.2
), (
57
), and (
58
) to conclude that there exist absolute constants
c_{8},c_{9}>0
such with probability at least
1-3\delta
,

\displaystyle\lambda\sqrt{\hat{s}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{8}\left(\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\hat{s}\sqrt{\log(epn/\hat{s})}+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\hat{s}^{3/4}\big(\log(1/\delta)\big)^{1/4}+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\hat{s}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+\hat{s}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+c_{9}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\sqrt{n\hat{s}\log(ep/\hat{s})+n\log(n/\delta)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+c_{9}(2+\bar{c})^{2}nL_{f}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})r^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+c_{9}(2+\bar{c})n\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\hat{s})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})r+c_{0}^{-1}\lambda\sqrt{\hat{s}}.

Set
m:=n/\log(np/\delta)
. Recall that
s\mapsto\varphi_{\max}(s)
is non-decreasing. By statement (i) and since
c_{0}>1
above inequality can be simplified to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lambda\sqrt{\hat{s}}&\leq\frac{c_{0}c_{8}}{c_{0}-1}\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(m)m}\sqrt{\hat{s}}\left(\sqrt{\log(ep/\hat{s})}+\left(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{m}\right)^{1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{m}}+1\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{c_{0}c_{9}}{c_{0}-1}\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(m)}\sqrt{\hat{s}}\left(\sqrt{n\log(ep/\hat{s})}+\sqrt{\frac{n\log(n/\delta)}{\hat{s}}}\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{c_{0}c_{9}}{c_{0}-1}\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})(2+\bar{c})^{2}L_{f}\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(m)}nr^{2}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{c_{0}c_{9}}{c_{0}-1}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})(2+\bar{c})\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(m)}nr.\end{split}

(59)

Divide above inequality by
\lambda=C_{\lambda}\frac{n}{\sqrt{m}}\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(m)\vee\widehat{\varphi}_{\max}(m)}
and conclude that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\hat{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep/\hat{s})}{n}}+\left(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n^{2}m}\right)^{1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{nm}}+n^{-1/2}\right)\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\hat{s}}}{C_{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\sqrt{\frac{m\log(ep/\hat{s})}{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\hat{s}}}{C_{\lambda}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\sqrt{\frac{m\log(n/\delta)}{C_{\lambda}^{2}n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{L_{f}}{C_{\lambda}}(2+\bar{c})^{2}\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{m}r^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{1}{C_{\lambda}}(2+\bar{c})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{m}r.

Since
m\log(p/\delta)=o(n)
, above inequality simplifies to

\displaystyle\sqrt{\hat{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{L_{f}}{C_{\lambda}}(2+\bar{c})^{2}\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{m}r^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\kappa_{1}(\bar{c})}{C_{\lambda}}\frac{\sqrt{m}r}{\sqrt{\varphi_{\max}(m)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\widetilde{C}_{1}^{2}}{C_{\lambda}}L_{f}(2+\bar{c})^{2}\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{\frac{s_{\theta}\log(np/\delta)}{n}}\sqrt{s_{\theta}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\widetilde{C}_{1}}{C_{\lambda}}(2+\bar{c})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{s_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{c_{0}}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\widetilde{C}_{1}}{C_{\lambda}}\left(L_{f}(2+\bar{c})^{2}\phi_{\max}(s_{\theta})+(2+\bar{c})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\right)\sqrt{s_{\theta}}.

This concludes the proof of the second statement.
∎

I.3
Proofs of Section
F.5

Proof of Lemma
8
.

The proof strategy is standard
(
Bickel et al., 2009
;
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2011
;
Belloni and Chernozhukov, 2013
, e.g.)
. By optimality of
\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)
and the premise, for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{\tau}\big(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{\tau}\big(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)+\lambda\|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\|_{1}-\lambda\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\tau-1\big\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)+\lambda\|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\|_{1}-\lambda\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\left(-c_{0}^{-1}\|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}+\|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda}(\tau)\|_{1}-\|\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left(1+c_{0}^{-1}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)-\theta_{0,k}(\tau)\big|\geq\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)-\theta_{0,k}(\tau)\big|+\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)\big|-\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\theta_{0,k}(\tau)\big|.

(60)

By Assumption
3
and the reverse triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)-\theta_{0,k}(\tau)\big|+\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)\big|-\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\theta_{0,k}(\tau)\big|\geq 2\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}^{c}(\tau)}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)\big|.

(61)

Combine eq. (
60
) and (
61
) to conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}(\tau)}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)-\theta_{0,k}\big|\geq\sum_{k\in T_{\theta}^{c}(\tau)}\big|\hat{\theta}_{\lambda,k}(\tau)\big|.

(62)

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

Obviously,
\phi_{\tau,x,y}(0)=0
and
|\phi_{\tau,x,y}(a)-\phi_{\tau,x,y}(b)|\leq|a-b|
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, by a change of variables,

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau,x,y}\big(x^{\prime}\theta-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{x^{\prime}\theta-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)}1\big\{y\leq x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)+u+\big(x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)\big\}du

\displaystyle=\int_{x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)}^{x^{\prime}\theta-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)}1\{y\leq x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)+u\}du

\displaystyle=\phi_{s,x,y}\big(x^{\prime}\theta-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)-\phi_{s,x,y}\big(x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big).

Lastly, re-arrange Knight’s identity and obtain

\displaystyle\rho_{\tau}(y-x^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}\big(y-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)=-\tau\big(x^{\prime}\theta-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)+\phi_{\tau,x,y}\big(x^{\prime}\theta-x^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big).

∎

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Proof of Case (i).

Define

\displaystyle L(\theta\mid X):=\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta)\mid X\right]=(\tau-1)\int_{-\infty}^{X^{\prime}\theta}(z-X^{\prime}\theta)dF_{Y|X}(z)+\tau\int_{X^{\prime}\theta}^{\infty}(z-X^{\prime}\theta)dF_{Y|X}(z).

Hence, by Leibniz’ rule for differentiating parameter integrals,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\theta}L(\theta\mid X)=(\tau-1)\int_{-\infty}^{X^{\prime}\theta}-X^{\prime}dF_{Y|X}(z)+\tau\int_{X^{\prime}\theta}^{\infty}-X^{\prime}dF_{Y|X}(z)=\left(F_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta|X)-\tau\right)X^{\prime},

(63)

and, by differentiating eq. (
63
),

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{d\theta d\theta^{\prime}}L(\theta\mid X)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta|X)XX^{\prime}.

(64)

By optimality of
\theta_{0}(\tau)
we have
\frac{d}{d\theta}L(\theta_{0}(\tau)\mid X)=0
. Hence, eq. (
63
), eq. (
64
), and a second order Taylor approximation around
\theta_{0}(\tau)
yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau))\mid X\right]=\frac{1}{2}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\xi(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big),

(65)

where
\xi(\tau)=\lambda\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)+\theta_{0}(\tau)
for some
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Whence, by Assumption
7
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\big]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\mathbb{E}[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\xi(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big/\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\big]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\geq\frac{\kappa_{1}(c_{0})}{2}r_{0}^{2}\inf_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\inf_{\|\zeta\|_{2}\leq r_{0}}\inf_{u\in C^{p}_{1}(T_{\theta}(\tau),c_{0})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,1)}\frac{\mathbb{E}[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)+X^{\prime}\zeta)|X)(X^{\prime}u)^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)(X^{\prime}u)^{2}]}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\gtrsim\frac{\kappa_{1}(c_{0})}{2}r_{0}^{2}.

This proves the first statement.

Proof of Case (ii).
By Assumption
5
the right hand side of eq. (
65
) can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)-\frac{L_{f}}{2}\big|X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\big|^{3}.

By Assumption
7′
there exists an absolute constant
c>1
such that
q_{1}(c_{0})(r_{0}/c)\leq 1/2
. Whence, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathcal{T}
such that
\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}1(J,c_{0})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,r_{0}/c)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\big]\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\left[1-\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{u\in C^{p}_{1}(T_{\theta}(\tau),c_{0})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,1)}\frac{L_{f}\mathbb{E}[|X^{\prime}u|^{3}]\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}}{\mathbb{E}[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)(X^{\prime}u)^{2}]}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\geq\frac{\kappa_{1}(c_{0})}{2}\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{c^{2}}\left[1-\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{u\in C^{p}_{1}(T_{\theta}(\tau),c_{0})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,1)}\frac{L_{f}\mathbb{E}[|X^{\prime}u|^{3}](r_{0}/c)}{\mathbb{E}[f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)(X^{\prime}u)^{2}]}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\gtrsim\frac{\kappa_{1}(c_{0})}{4}r_{0}^{2}.

This proves the second statement.

Proof of Case (iii).
Let
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathcal{T}
be such that
\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}_{1}(J,c_{0})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,r_{0})
. Then, by Assumption
7′′
there exists an absolute constant
\underline{f}>0
such that the right hand side of eq. (
65
) can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle(\underline{f}/2)\Big(X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big)^{2}\mathbf{1}\left\{|X^{\prime}(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau))|\leq 2q_{0}(c_{0})r_{0}\right\},

and, therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta)-\rho_{\tau}(Y-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\geq(\underline{f}/2)\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big)^{2}\right]-(\underline{f}/2)\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big)^{2}\mathbf{1}\left\{\Big|X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big|>2q_{0}(c_{0})r_{0}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\geq(\underline{f}/2)\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big)^{2}\right]-(\underline{f}/2)\mathbb{E}\left[\big|X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\big|^{3}\right]/(2q_{0}(c_{0})r_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\overset{(a)}{\geq}(\underline{f}/4)\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\gtrsim(\underline{f}/4)\kappa_{0}(c_{0})r_{0}^{2},

where (a) holds because for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathcal{T}
with
\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}_{1}(J,c_{0})\cap\partial B^{p}(0,r_{0})
,

\displaystyle q_{0}(c_{0})r_{0}\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\big|X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\big|^{3}\right]\Big/\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(X^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\Big)^{2}\right].

This proves the third statement.
∎

Proof of Lemma
11
.

By Lemma 7.1 (ii) in
Koltchinskii, 2011
the set
\mathcal{M}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
is such that
C^{p}_{1}(J,\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1)\subset 2(2+\vartheta)\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M})
,
\|u\|_{0}\leq s
for all
u\in\mathcal{M}
, and
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\leq 5^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{s}{p\choose k}
. By Proposition 3.6.4 in
Giné and Nickl, 2015
we have
\sum_{k=0}^{s}{p\choose k}\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{p^{s}}{s!}\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{ep}{s}\right)^{s}
for
p\geq s+2
. A trivial modification of Koltchinskii’s arguments yields the claim of the lemma for all
q\geq 1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
12
.

For each
x\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X})
there exist
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\geq 0
,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}=1
, such that
x=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}x_{i}
for some
x_{i}\in\mathcal{X}
. Similarly, for each
y\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{Y})
there exist
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{m}\geq 0
,
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}=1
, such that
y=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{i}y_{i}
for some
y_{i}\in\mathcal{Y}
. Thus, by biconvexity of
f
and two applications of Jensen’s inequality, for all
x\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X})
and for all
y\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{Y})
,

\displaystyle f(x,y)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\mu_{j}f(x_{i},y_{j})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\mu_{j}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}f(x,y)=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}f(x,y).

Thus,
\sup_{x\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X})}\sup_{y\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{Y})}f(x,y)\leq\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\sup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}f(x,y)
. The reverse inequality holds trivially true since
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{Y})
. The same arguments hold if
f
is replaced by
|f|
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
13
.

We plan to apply the maximal inequality from Theorem
8
. However, instead of applying Theorem
8
to the original process, we apply it to various auxiliary processes. We then use Lemma
38
to assemble a bound for the original empirical process from the bounds of those auxiliary processes. The rationale behind this complicated approach is that a direct application of Theorem
8
leads to an upper bound involving the metric entropy integral over
C^{p}(\cup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}T_{\theta}(\tau),c_{0})\cap B^{p}(0,r_{0})
, for which we lack the tools to obtain tight estimates. In contrast, the bounds on the auxiliary processes involve metric entropy integrals of finite sets only which are easy to estimate.

Denote the underlying probability space by
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})
. To simplify notation, write
K(r_{0},\tau)=C^{p}(T_{\theta}(\tau),c_{0})\cap B^{p}(0,r_{0})
. Let
\eta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
be an
\eta
-net with cardinality
\mathrm{card}(T_{\eta})\leq 1+1/\eta
. We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{u\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{\prime}u-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\prime}u]\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{\tau,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{\tau,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)\big]\right|

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{u\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{\prime}u-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\prime}u]\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{s\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)\big]\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{s\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)\big]\right|

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}+\mathbf{III},

(66)

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
9

(iii)
and
(b)
follows from Lemma
9

(ii)
.

Bound on
\mathbf{I}
.
By Lemma
11
and Assumption
3
there exists
\mathcal{M}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
with cardinality
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s_{\theta}}\right)^{s_{\theta}}
,
\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{\theta}
for all
u\in\mathcal{M}
, and
K(1,\tau)\subset 2(2+c_{0})\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M})
for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
. Hence, for
A\in\mathcal{A}
arbitrary,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{u\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{\prime}u-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\prime}u]\right|\mid A\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2(2+c_{0})r_{0}\>\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{\prime}u-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\prime}u]\right|\mid A\right].

Note that
u\mapsto\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}])^{\prime}u
is linear. Hence, by Lemma
12
the term on the right hand side in above display is equal to

\displaystyle 2(2+c_{0})r_{0}\>\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{M}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{\prime}u-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{\prime}u]\right|\mid A\right].

(67)

Now, note that for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\big\|(X-\mathbb{E}[X])^{\prime}u_{1}-(X-\mathbb{E}[X])^{\prime}u_{2}\big\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}.

Thus, by Theorem
8
and Remark
18
, eq. (
67
) can be upper bounded (up to a multiplicative constant) by

\displaystyle 2(2+c_{0})r_{0}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\mathbb{P}\{A\})}.

Hence, by Lemma
37
, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\lesssim 2(2+c_{0})r_{0}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\left(\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})}+\sqrt{t}\right).

(68)

Bound on
\mathbf{II}
.
Let
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. Rademacher random variables independent of
\{(Y_{i},X_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
. For
A\in\mathcal{A}
arbitrary consider

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(s)\big)\right|\mid A\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\>\sup_{u\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}u\right|\mid A\right]

(69)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\left|\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}\big(\theta_{0}(\tau)-\theta_{0}(s)\big)\right|\mid A\right].

(70)

Note that the symmetrized summands
\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}u
have zero mean, are sub-Gaussian, and have the same second moments as
(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}u
. Hence,
\|\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}u\|_{P,\psi_{2}}\lesssim\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(\|u\|_{0})\|u\|_{2}
. Proceeding as in Step 1, we can therefore upper bound the term in eq. (
69
) (up to a multiplicative constant) by

\displaystyle 16(2+c_{0})r_{0}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\mathbb{P}\{A\})}.

Note that
\sup_{\tau,s\in\mathcal{T}}\|\theta(\tau)-\theta(s)\|_{0}\leq 2s_{\theta}
. Hence, by Assumption
4
, for all
\tau,s\in\mathcal{T}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\varepsilon\big(X-\mathbb{E}[X]\big)^{\prime}\theta(\tau)-\varepsilon\big(X-\mathbb{E}[X]\big)^{\prime}\theta(s)\big\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\|\theta(\tau)-\theta(s)\|_{2}\lesssim\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}|\tau-s|.

Therefore, by Theorem
8
, Remark
17
, and Remark
18
, eq. (
70
) can be upper bounded (up to a multiplicative constant) by

\displaystyle 2(2+c_{0})\eta\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}\sqrt{2s_{\theta}\log(ep/2s_{\theta})+\log(1/\mathbb{P}\{A\})}.

Thus, by Lemma
37
, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{8}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(s)\big)\right|\\
&\lesssim 16(2+c_{0})r_{0}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\left(\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})}+\sqrt{t}\right)+16(2+c_{0})\eta\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}\left(\sqrt{2s_{\theta}\log(ep/2s_{\theta})}+\sqrt{t}\right)\end{split}

(71)

We now turn the bound on this symmetrized process into a bound on
\mathbf{II}
. For any increasing and convex function
F
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)\big]\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(2\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\phi_{s,X_{i},Y_{i}}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(4\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(8\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\>\sup_{\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\in K(r_{0},\tau)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\big(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\big)^{\prime}\big(\theta-\theta_{0}(s)\big)\right|\right)\right]

(72)

where
(a)
holds by Lemma 2.3.6 in
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
,
(b)
holds by Theorem 4.12 in
Ledoux and Talagrand, 1996
and since by Lemma
9
(i)
\phi_{s,X,Y}
is a contraction, and
(c)
holds again by Lemma 2.3.6 in
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
.

By Lemma
38
, eq. (
71
) and (
72
), and the union bound over
\eta\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-2e^{1-t}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{II}&\lesssim 16(2+c_{0})r_{0}\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\left(\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})}+\sqrt{\log(1+1/\eta)+t}\right)\\
&+16(2+c_{0})\eta\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}\left(\sqrt{2s_{\theta}\log(ep/2s_{\theta})}+\sqrt{\log(1+1/\eta)+t}\right).\end{split}

(73)

Bound on
\mathbf{III}
.
We obtain a bound for this term using similar arguments as in Step 2. We skip the redundant details and simply note that, for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{1-t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{III}\lesssim 16(2+c_{0})\eta\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})L_{\theta}\left(\sqrt{2s_{\theta}\log(ep/2s_{\theta})}+\sqrt{\log(1+1/\eta)+t}\right).

(74)

Conclusion.
Since
\eta\in(0,1)
is arbitrary, we can choose
\eta\asymp r_{0}/L_{\theta}
. Combine the bounds in eq. (
I.3
), (
68
), (
73
) and (
74
), adjust the constants, and conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\lesssim 2(2+c_{0})\phi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})r_{0}\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1+L_{\theta}/r_{0})+\log(1/\delta)}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
14
.

To simplify notation, we write
K_{k}(\tau)=C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}(0,1)
for
k\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof of Case (i).
By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M}_{k})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s_{k}}\right)^{s_{k}},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall u\in\mathcal{M}_{k}:\>\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{k},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau\in\mathcal{T},\>k\in\{1,2\}:\>K_{k}(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta_{k})\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M}_{k}).

For these
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}}=\big\{g(X,\xi)=\xi(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2}):\>u_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{k},\>k\in\{1,2\}\big\}.

By Assumption
1
and Lemma
36
, for all
v_{1},u_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
v_{2},u_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\big(\xi(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2})-\mathbb{E}[\xi(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2})]\big)-\big(\xi(X^{\prime}v_{1})(X^{\prime}v_{2})-\mathbb{E}[\xi(X^{\prime}v_{1})(X^{\prime}v_{2})]\big)\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\left\|\xi(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2})-\xi(X^{\prime}v_{1})(X^{\prime}v_{2})\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\sup_{w_{1},w_{2}}\left\|(X^{\prime}w_{1})(X^{\prime}w_{2})\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\left(\|u_{1}-v_{1}\|_{2}+\|u_{2}-v_{2}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\left(\|u_{1}-v_{1}\|_{2}+\|u_{2}-v_{2}\|_{2}\right),

where the supremum in the third line is taken over all
w_{1},w_{2}
such that
\|w_{k}\|_{2}\leq 1
and
\|w_{k}\|_{0}\leq s_{k}
for
k\in\{1,2\}
. Thus, by Corollary
4
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\left(\sqrt{t_{s_{1},s_{2},\delta}}+n^{-1/2}t_{s_{1},s_{2},\delta}\right),

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+\log(1/\delta)
. Hence, by Lemma
12
and construction of
\mathcal{M}
we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\left(\sqrt{t_{s_{1},s_{2},\delta}}+n^{-1/2}t_{s_{1},s_{2},\delta}\right).

Proof of Case (ii).
Let
\mathcal{M}_{k}
be as in the proof of case (i) and define
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k}=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:u\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\mathrm{\>or\>}-u\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\}
. Then,
\mathrm{card}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k})\leq 2\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M}_{k})
,
\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{k}
for all
u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k}
and
K(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta_{k})\mathrm{conv}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k})
. The claim follows now by the same arguments used to proof case (i).

Proof of Case (iii).
The claim is a simple consequence from the fact that the proofs of cases (i) and (ii) rely on an
\varepsilon
-net approximation of
s
-sparse sets. In fact, the proofs of these cases establish case (iii) and then use Lemma
11
to deduce the case of
v\in C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1)
. This completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Lemma
15
.

To simplify notation, we write
K(\tau)=C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1)
.

Proof of Case (i).
By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{M}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s}\right)^{s},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall u\in\mathcal{M}:\>\|u\|_{0}\leq s,\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau\in\mathcal{T}:\>K(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta)\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M}).

For this
\mathcal{M}
we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle=\left\{g(X,Y,\xi)=\xi\left(\tau-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v:\>v\in\mathcal{M},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

We observe the following: First,
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{M}}=\{hj:h\in\mathcal{H},\>j\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\}
, where
\mathcal{H}=\big\{h(X,Y)=\tau-\mathbf{1}\{F_{Y|X}(Y|X)\leq\tau\}:\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}=\{j(X,\xi)=\xi X^{\prime}v:v\in\mathcal{M}\}
. The set
\mathcal{H}
is the difference of two VC-subgraph classes with VC-indices at most 2, respectively
(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.15 and Example 2.6.1)
. Thus,
\mathcal{H}
is VC-subgraph class with VC-index at most
3

(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.18)
. The function class
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}
is finite with
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})=\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})
. By Assumption
1
and Lemma
36
, for
u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
arbitrary,

\displaystyle\left\|\xi^{2}(u_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}u_{2}-\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}u_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}u_{2}])-(\xi^{2}v_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v_{2}-\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}v_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v_{2}])\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\left\|\xi^{2}u_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}u_{2}-\xi^{2}v_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v_{2}\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\sup_{w_{1},w_{2}}\left\|(X^{\prime}w_{1})(X^{\prime}w_{2})\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\left(\|u_{1}-v_{1}\|_{2}+\|u_{2}-v_{2}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}(|S|)\left(\|u_{1}-v_{1}\|_{2}+\|u_{2}-v_{2}\|_{2}\right),

where the supremum in the third line is taken over all
w_{1},w_{2}
such that
\|w_{1}\|_{2},\|w_{2}\|_{2}\leq 1
and
\|w_{1}\|_{0},\|w_{2}\|_{0}\leq|S|
. Therefore, for
j_{v},j_{u}\in\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}
,

\displaystyle\|(j_{v}^{2}-Pj_{v}^{2})-(j_{u}^{2}-Pj_{u}^{2})\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}(|S|)\|v-u\|_{2}.

Thus, by Corollary
5
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\sqrt{t_{s,\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}(t_{s,\delta})}

where
t_{s,\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+\log(1/\delta)
and
\pi_{n,1}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z/n
for
z\geq 0
. Hence, by Lemma
12
and construction of
\mathcal{M}
we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\lesssim 2(2+\vartheta)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\sqrt{t_{s,\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}(t_{s,\delta})}.

Proof of Case (ii).
The claim is a simple consequence from the fact that the proof of case (i) uses an
\varepsilon
-net approximation of
s
-sparse sets. In fact, the proof of case (i) establish first case (ii) and then invokes Lemma
11
to deduce the case of
v\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1)
. This completes the proof.

Proof of Case (iii).
Repeat the proofs of cases (i) and (ii) but use the
\psi_{1/2}
-Orlicz norm to bound the increments and apply Corollary
5
with
\alpha=1/2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
16
.

Proof of Case (i).
Let
S\subset\{1,\ldots,p\}
be arbitrary. By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{M}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{|S|}\right)^{|S|},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall u\in\mathcal{M}:\>\|u\|_{0}\leq|S|,\hskip 9.24994pt{}B^{p}(0,1)\subset 4\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M}).

In the following it is understood that
\mathcal{M}
is a function of
S
and we will not make this dependence explicit. For
T\subset\{1,\ldots,p\}
and the pair
(S,\mathcal{M})
we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle=\left\{g(X,Y,\xi)=\xi\left(\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\big\}-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v:\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\mathrm{supp}(\theta)=T,\>v\in\mathcal{M},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

We make the following observations: First, each
g\in\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}
is parameterized (uniquely) by the triple
(v,\theta,\tau)
. Hence, we will write
g=g_{v,\theta,\tau}
whenever we need to highlight the dependence on the parameters. Second,
\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}=\{hj:h\in\mathcal{H}_{T},\>j\in\mathcal{J}_{S,\mathcal{M}}\}
, where
\mathcal{H}_{T}=\big\{h(X,Y)=\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\}-\mathbf{1}\{F_{Y|X}(Y|X)\leq\tau\}:\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\mathrm{supp}(\theta)=T,\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}
and
\mathcal{J}_{S,\mathcal{M}}=\{j(X,\xi)=\xi X^{\prime}v:v\in\mathcal{M}\}
. In particular, for every
g=g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}
there exist unique
h_{\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{H}_{T}
and
j_{v}\in\mathcal{J}_{S,\mathcal{M}}
such that
g_{v,\theta,\tau}=h_{\theta,\tau}j_{v}
. The set
\mathcal{H}_{T}
is the difference of two VC-subgraph classes with VC-indices at most
|T|+3

(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.15)
and 2
(
Dudley, 2014
, Theorem 4.10 (a))
, respectively. Thus,
\mathcal{H}_{T}
is VC-subgraph class with VC-index at most
|T|+4

(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.18)
. The function class
\mathcal{J}_{S,\mathcal{M}}
is finite with
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{J}_{S,\mathcal{M}})=\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})
. By Assumption
1
and Lemma
36
, for
u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
arbitrary,

\displaystyle\left\|\xi^{2}(u_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}u_{2}-\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}u_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}u_{2}])-(\xi^{2}v_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v_{2}-\mathbb{E}[\xi^{2}v_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v_{2}])\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\left\|\xi^{2}u_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}u_{2}-\xi^{2}v_{1}^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v_{2}\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\sup_{w_{1},w_{2}}\left\|(X^{\prime}w_{1})(X^{\prime}w_{2})\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\left(\|u_{1}-v_{1}\|_{2}+\|u_{2}-v_{2}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}(|S|)\left(\|u_{1}-v_{1}\|_{2}+\|u_{2}-v_{2}\|_{2}\right),

where the supremum in the third line is taken over all
w_{1},w_{2}
such that
\|w_{1}\|_{2},\|w_{2}\|_{2}\leq 1
and
\|w_{1}\|_{0},\|w_{2}\|_{0}\leq|S|
. Therefore, for
j_{v},j_{u}\in\mathcal{J}_{S,\mathcal{M}}
,

\displaystyle\|(j_{v}^{2}-Pj_{v}^{2})-(j_{u}^{2}-Pj_{u}^{2})\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}(|S|)\|v-u\|_{2}.

Thus, by Corollary
5
there exists an absolute constant
C_{1}>0
(independent of
S,T,n,p,\mathcal{M}
) such that for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}}>C_{1}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|)\Pi_{n,p}(|S|,|T|,t)\right\}\leq 3e^{1-t},

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{n,p}(|S|,|T|,t)=\left(\sqrt{|T|+|S|\log(ep/|S|)}+\sqrt{t}\right)\left(\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}\big(|S|\log(ep/|S|)\big)}+\sqrt{\pi_{n,1}^{2}(t)}\right),

and
\pi_{n,1}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z/n
for
z\geq 0
.

Note that
\mathrm{card}\big(\{S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}:|S|\leq k\}\big)\leq\left(ep/k\right)^{k}
and
\mathrm{card}\big(\{T\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}:|T|\leq\ell\}\big)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}{p\choose i}\leq\left(ep/\ell\right)^{\ell}
. Therefore, setting
t_{k,\ell,n,\delta}=k\log(ep/k)+\ell\log(ep/\ell)+2\log n+\log(3e/\delta)
and applying the union bound over above tail probabilities gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{1\leq k,\ell\leq n}\sup_{\mathrm{card}(S)\leq k}\sup_{\mathrm{card}(T)\leq\ell}\frac{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}}}{\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(k)\Pi_{n,p}(k,\ell,t_{k,\ell,n,\delta})}>C_{1}\right\}\leq 3e\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(\frac{ep}{k}\right)^{k}\left(\frac{ep}{\ell}\right)^{\ell}e^{-t_{k,\ell,n,\delta}}\leq\delta.

(75)

Next, for
S,T\subset\{1,\ldots,p\}
arbitrary, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{S,T}=\left\{g(X,Y,\xi)=\xi\left(\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\big\}-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v:\right.

\displaystyle\left.\theta,v\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\mathrm{supp}(\theta)=T,\>\mathrm{supp}(v)=S,\>\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

By Lemma
12
and construction of
\mathcal{M}
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,T}}\leq 4\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,T,\mathcal{M}}},

(76)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\bigcup_{S,T\subset\{1,\ldots p\},\>\mathrm{card}(S),\mathrm{card}(T)\leq n}\mathcal{G}_{S,T}.

(77)

Hence, eq. (
75
)–(
77
) imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}:\frac{\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{v,\theta,\tau})}{\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(\|v\|_{0})\Pi_{n,p}(\|v\|_{0},\|\theta\|_{0},t_{\|v\|_{0},\|\theta\|_{0},n,\delta})}>4C_{1}\right\}\leq\delta.
