Proof of Lemma
23
.

For notational convenience, we write
v_{0}(\tau)
,
\tilde{v}(\tau)
, and
T_{v}(\tau)
for
v_{0}(\tau;z)
,
\tilde{v}(\tau;z)
, and
T_{v}(\tau;z)
, respectively.

To prove part (i), compute

\displaystyle\|v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq r_{a}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}r_{a}^{k}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2}=\frac{r_{a}}{1-r_{a}}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2},

where (a) holds by iterating
\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq\|v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2}+\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{a}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}+\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2}
.
To prove part (ii), write

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}MM^{\prime}\big(\tilde{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=\|\tilde{v}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}+\|v_{0}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}-2\big(M^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\big(M^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)\big),

(151)

and upper bound the right-hand side in above identity as follows:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{v}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}+\|v_{0}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}-2\big(M^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\big(M^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=(1-r_{a}^{2})\left[\left(\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\right)^{2}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}\right)^{2}\|v_{0}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{2}{1-r_{a}^{2}}\big(M^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\big(M^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)\big)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+(1-r_{a}^{2})\left[\frac{1}{1-r_{a}^{2}}-\left(\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\right)^{2}\right]\|\tilde{v}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}+(1-r_{a}^{2})\left[\frac{1}{1-r_{a}^{2}}-\left(\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}\right)^{2}\right]\|v_{0}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq(1-r_{a}^{2})\left(\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}v_{0}(\tau)\right)^{\prime}MM^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}v_{0}(\tau)\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\frac{2r_{a}}{1+r_{a}}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)^{\prime}M\|_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(152)

Next, by construction of
\tilde{v}(\tau)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}v_{0}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}\right)\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}\big(v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{-2r_{a}}{1-r_{a}^{2}}\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}\big(v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)

(153)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}\left[\frac{-2r_{a}}{1+r_{a}}\big(v_{0,T}(\tau),0_{T^{c}}\big)+\big(0_{T},v_{0,T^{c}}(\tau)\big)\right].

(154)

From eq. (
153
) we infer that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}v_{0}(\tau)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2r_{a}}{1-r_{a}^{2}}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{2}+\frac{r_{a}}{1-r_{a}}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{a}\frac{3+r_{a}}{1-r_{a}^{2}}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2},

(155)

and from eq. (
154
) we learn that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+r_{a}}\tilde{v}(\tau)-\frac{1}{1-r_{a}}v_{0}(\tau)\>\>\in\>\>C_{2}^{p}(T_{v}(\tau),1)

(156)

because
\frac{2r_{a}}{1+r_{a}}\geq\frac{r_{a}}{1-r_{a}}
for all
0\leq r_{a}\leq 1/3
and Lemma
22
applied with
\alpha=\beta=\frac{r_{a}}{1-r_{a}}
.

Combining (
155
) and (
156
) we upper bound (
I.7
) by

\displaystyle(1-r_{a}^{2})\sup_{u\in C_{2}^{p}(T,1)\cap B_{2}^{p}(0,\alpha\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2})}u^{\prime}MM^{\prime}u+\sup_{u\in C_{2}^{p}(T,1)\cap B_{2}^{p}(0,\alpha\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2})}u^{\prime}MM^{\prime}u,

where
\alpha=r_{a}\frac{3+r_{a}}{1-r_{a}^{2}}
. To conclude the proof of part (ii) note that for
0\leq r_{a}\leq 1/3
we can upper bound
(2-r_{a}^{2})\alpha<8r_{a}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
23
.

For notational convenience, we write
v_{0}(\tau)
,
\tilde{v}(\tau)
, and
T_{v}(\tau)
for
v_{0}(\tau;z)
,
\tilde{v}(\tau;z)
, and
T_{v}(\tau;z)
, respectively.
By construction of
\tilde{v}(\tau)
,

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k\in T^{c}_{v}(\tau)}|v_{0,k}(\tau)|^{2}\right)^{1/2}=\|\tilde{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{k\in T^{c}_{v}(\tau)}|v_{0,k}(\tau)|^{2}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau)}|v_{0,k}(\tau)|^{2}\right)^{1/2},

and, hence,

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k\in T^{c}_{v}(\tau)}|v_{0,k}(\tau)|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau)}|v_{0,k}(\tau)|^{2}\right)^{1/2},

i.e.
v_{0}(\tau)\in C^{p}_{2}(T_{v}(\tau),1)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\tilde{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\in U^{p}(\tau):=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:(u_{T_{v}(\tau)},u_{T_{v}^{c}(\tau)})=(0,v_{T_{v}^{c}(\tau)}),\>v\in C^{p}_{2}(T_{v}(\tau),1)\}.

Thus, since
MM^{\prime}
is positive semi-definite,

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}MM^{\prime}\big(\tilde{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\big)\leq\sup_{u\in U^{p}(\tau)}u^{\prime}MM^{\prime}u\leq\sup_{u\in C^{p}_{2}(T_{v}(\tau),1)}u^{\prime}MM^{\prime}u.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
24
.

For notational convenience, we write
v_{0}(\tau)
,
\tilde{v}(\tau)
,
\hat{v}(\tau)
,
f(\tau)
,
f_{i}(\tau)
, and
T_{v}(\tau)
for
v_{0}(\tau;z)
,
\tilde{v}(\tau;z)
,
\hat{v}_{\gamma}(\tau;z)
,
f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)
,
f_{Y|X}(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X_{i})
, and
T_{v}(\tau;z)
, respectively.

By optimality of
\hat{v}(\tau)
and Lemma
23
, for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{v}(\tau)\big)^{2}+nz^{\prime}\hat{v}(\tau)+\gamma\|\hat{v}(\tau)\|_{1}-\left(\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)^{2}+nz^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)+\gamma\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\left(\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\hat{v}(\tau)\big)^{2}-\big(X_{i}^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)\big)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\left(\big(X_{i}^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)\big)^{2}-\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+nz^{\prime}\big(\hat{v}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)+\gamma\|\hat{v}(\tau)\|_{1}-\gamma\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)X_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)\right)^{\prime}\big(\hat{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\big)+\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)X_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}\tilde{v}(\tau)\right)^{\prime}\big(v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+nz^{\prime}\big(\hat{v}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)+\gamma\|\hat{v}(\tau)\|_{1}-\gamma\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)X_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}v_{0}(\tau)+nz\right)^{\prime}\big(\hat{v}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)+\gamma\|\hat{v}(\tau)\|_{1}-\gamma\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}-\big(v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{f}_{i}^{2}(\tau)X_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}\right)\big(v_{0}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\gamma\left(-c_{0}^{-1}\|\hat{v}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{1}+\|\hat{v}(\tau)\|_{1}-\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{1}-\gamma^{-1}\mu c_{0}^{-1}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left(1+c_{0}^{-1}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|+c_{0}^{-1}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|+\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|-\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|.

(157)

By Assumption
10
and the reverse triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|+\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|-\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big|\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|\geq 2\sum_{k\in T_{v}^{c}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|.

(158)

Combine eq. (
157
) and (
158
) and note that
\|v_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq\|z\|_{2}/\kappa_{2}(\infty)
to conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|+\frac{1}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\frac{\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\geq\sum_{k\in T_{v}^{c}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|.

This completes the proof of the first statement of the lemma.

Next, if
\frac{1}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\frac{\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\leq\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|
, then

\displaystyle 2\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|\geq\sum_{k\in T_{v}^{c}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|,

whereas if
\frac{1}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\frac{\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}>\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}\sum_{k\in T_{v}(\tau)}\big|\hat{v}_{k}(\tau)-\tilde{v}_{k}(\tau)\big|
, then

\displaystyle 2\frac{c_{0}+1}{c_{0}-1}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\frac{\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\geq\|\hat{v}(\tau)-v_{0}(\tau)\|_{1}.

Thus, we conclude that

\displaystyle\hat{v}(\tau)-\tilde{v}(\tau)\in C^{p}_{1}(T_{v}(\tau),2\bar{c})\cup B^{p}_{1}\left(0,\frac{2\bar{c}\|z\|_{2}^{2}}{\kappa_{2}^{2}(\infty)}\frac{\mu}{\gamma}\right).

This completes the proof of the second statement of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
25
.

Let
s,\tau\in\mathcal{T}
be arbitrary. For notational convenience, we introduce
D_{2}(\tau):=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)XX^{\prime}\right]
and write
s_{v}
for
s_{v}(z)
.

\displaystyle\left\|v_{0}(s;z)-v_{0}(\tau;z)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\|D_{2}^{-1}(s)\|_{op}\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1,\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{v}}\big\|\big(D_{2}(\tau)-D_{2}(s)\big)u\|_{2}\|D_{2}^{-1}(\tau)z\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\frac{2\|z\|_{2}}{\kappa_{2}(\infty)}\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1,\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{v}}\sup_{\|v\|_{2}\leq 1}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[v^{\prime}\big(f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)|X)-f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\big)XX^{\prime}u\right]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\frac{2\bar{f}L_{f}L_{\theta}\|z\|_{2}}{\kappa_{2}(\infty)}\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1,\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{v}}\sup_{\|v\|_{2}\leq 1}\sup_{\|w\|_{2}\leq 1,\>\|w\|_{0}\leq 2s_{\theta}}\mathbb{E}|X^{\prime}w||u^{\prime}XX^{\prime}v|s-\tau|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\frac{2\bar{f}L_{f}L_{\theta}\|z\|_{2}}{\kappa_{2}(\infty)}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(p)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{v})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})|s-\tau|.

To conclude, simplify the bound.
∎

Proof of Lemma
26
.

We generalize the proof of Lemma
12
from
K=2
to arbitrary
K\in\mathbb{N}
.

For each
x_{j}\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X}_{j})
,
1\leq j\leq K
, there exist
n_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\lambda_{j}^{1},\ldots,\lambda_{j}^{n}\geq 0
,
\sum_{i_{j}=1}^{n_{j}}\lambda_{j}^{i_{j}}=1
, such that
x_{j}=\sum_{i_{j}=1}^{n_{j}}\lambda_{j}^{i_{j}}x_{j}^{i_{j}}
for some
x_{j}^{i_{j}}\in\mathcal{X}_{j}
. Thus, by the multi-convexity of the
f_{i}
’s and
N\times K
applications of Jensen’s inequality, for all
x_{j}\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X}_{j})
,
1\leq j\leq K
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}f_{\ell}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{i_{j}=1}^{n_{j}}\lambda_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots\lambda_{K}^{i_{K}}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}f_{\ell}(x_{1}^{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{K}^{i_{K}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{i_{j}=1}^{n_{j}}\lambda_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots\lambda_{K}^{i_{K}}\left(\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j},1\leq j\leq K}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}f_{\ell}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j},1\leq j\leq K}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}f_{\ell}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K}).

Thus,
\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X}_{j}),1\leq j\leq K}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})\leq\sup_{x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j},1\leq j\leq K}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})
. The reverse inequality holds trivially true since
\mathcal{X}_{j}\subseteq\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{X}_{j})
,
1\leq j\leq K
. The same arguments hold if
\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}
is replaced by
\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\right|
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
27
.

This lemma is proved as Lemma
15
(iii) with
\psi_{1/2}
replaced with
\psi_{1/3}
. We omit the repetitive details.
∎

Proof of Lemma
28
.

To simplify notation, we write
K_{k}(\tau)=C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}(0,1)
for
k\in\{1,2\}
and
f_{i}(\tau)=f_{Y|X}(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Proof of Case (i).
Let
\eta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
be an
\eta
-net with cardinality
\mathrm{card}(T_{\eta})\leq 1+1/\eta
. We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sup_{s\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}^{2}(s)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})-\mathbb{E}[f_{i}^{2}(s)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sup_{s\in T_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}^{2}(\tau)-f_{i}^{2}(s)\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(f_{i}^{2}(\tau)-f_{i}^{2}(s)\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

(159)

Bound on
\mathbf{I}
.
By Lemma
14
(ii) and the union bound over
s\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\lesssim 4(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}^{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\pi_{n,1}^{2}\big(t_{s_{1},s_{2},\eta,\delta}\big),

(160)

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},\eta,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+\log(1+1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
and
\pi_{n,1}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z/n
for
z\geq 0
.

Bound on
\mathbf{II}
.
We begin with deriving an upper bound on the supremum of an empirical processes indexed by an auxiliary function class. By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mathcal{W}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M}_{k})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s_{k}}\right)^{s_{k}},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall u\in\mathcal{M}_{k}:\>\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{k},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau\in\mathcal{T},\>k\in\{1,2\}:\>K_{k}(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta_{k})\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M}_{k});

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{W})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{2s_{\theta}}\right)^{2s_{\theta}},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall w\in\mathcal{W}:\>\|w\|_{0}\leq 2s_{\theta},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}:\>C^{p}(T_{\theta}(\tau)\cup T_{\theta}(\tau^{\prime}),0)\cap B^{p}(0,1)\subset 4\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{W}).

Let
\{g_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard normal random variables independent of
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\big\{f(X,g)=g(X^{\prime}u_{1})(X^{\prime}u_{2})X^{\prime}w:u_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{k},\>w\in\mathcal{W},\>k\in\{1,2\}\big\}

Next, we verify that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies the Lipschitz-type condition eq. (
43
). For
\alpha\in(0,1)
the
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm is a quasi-norm since
x\mapsto\exp(x^{\alpha})-1
is not convex for small values of
x
. However, by Assumption
1
and Lemma
36
there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that for all
u_{1},v_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{1}
,
u_{2},v_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
, and
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathcal{W}
,

\displaystyle\left\|gX^{\prime}u_{1}X^{\prime}v_{1}X^{\prime}w_{1}-\varepsilon X^{\prime}u_{2}X^{\prime}v_{2}X^{\prime}w_{2}\right\|_{P,\psi_{1/2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq C\sup_{u\in\mathcal{M}_{1}}\sup_{v\in\mathcal{M}_{2}}\sup_{w\in\mathcal{W}}\|X^{\prime}u\|_{P,\psi_{2}}\|X^{\prime}u\|_{P,\psi_{2}}\|X^{\prime}w\|_{P,\psi_{2}}\big(\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}+\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{2}+\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim C\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\big(\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}+\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{2}+\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}\big).

Whence, by Corollary
4
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}&\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})n^{-1/2}\Big(s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})\Big)^{2}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\left(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+n^{-1/2}\big(\log(1/\delta)\big)^{2}\right).\end{split}

(161)

We now turn the bound on
\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}
into a bound on
\mathbf{II}
. For any increasing and convex function
F
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}^{2}(\tau)-f_{i}^{2}(s)\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big(f_{i}^{2}(\tau)-f_{i}^{2}(s)\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})\right]\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sqrt{2\pi}\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\big(f_{i}^{2}(\tau)-f_{i}^{2}(s)\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(4\sqrt{2\pi}\bar{f}L_{f}\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(32\sqrt{2\pi}(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}L_{f}L_{\theta}\eta\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\right]

(162)

where
(a)
holds by Lemma 6.3 followed by Lemma 4.5 in
Ledoux and Talagrand, 1996
,
(b)
holds by Lemma
39
(note that the map
z\mapsto f_{Y|X}^{2}(z-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)|X)-f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s)|X)
is Lipschitz-continuous and vanishes at 0), and
(c)
holds by Assumption
4
, Lemma
26
, and construction of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
,
\mathcal{M}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{W}
.

By Lemma
38
(with
\alpha=1/2
and
\varphi(t)=t^{1/4}+n^{-1/2}t
), eq. (
161
) and (
162
), and the union bound over
\eta\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{II}&\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}L_{f}L_{\theta}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\left(\sqrt{s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})}\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}+n^{-1/2}\Big(s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})\Big)^{2}\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+n^{-1/2}\big(\log(1/\delta)\big)^{2}\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}\times\eta.\end{split}

(163)

Conclusion.
Since
\eta\in(0,1)
is arbitrary, we can choose
\eta\asymp 1/(L_{f}L_{\theta}n)
. Combine the bounds in eq. (
I.7
), (
160
), and (
163
), adjust the constants, and conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}^{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta}))\psi_{n}\big(t_{s_{\theta},s_{1},s_{2},n,\delta}\big),

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(nL_{f}L_{\theta}/\delta)
and
\psi_{n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(1+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{z}+n^{-3/2}z^{3/2}\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

Proof of Case (ii).
Let
\mathcal{M}_{k}
be as in the proof of case (i) and define
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k}=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:u\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\mathrm{\>or\>}-u\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\}
. Then,
\mathrm{card}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k})\leq 2\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M}_{k})
,
\|u\|_{0}\leq s_{k}
for all
u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k}
and
K(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta_{k})\mathrm{conv}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{k})
. The claim follows now by the same arguments used to proof case (i).

Proof of Case (iii).
The claim is a simple consequence from the fact that the proofs of cases (i) and (ii) rely on an
\varepsilon
-net approximation of
s
-sparse sets. In fact, the proofs of these cases establish case (iii) and then use Lemma
11
to deduce the case of
v\in C^{p}_{q_{k}}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
29
.

The proof strategy is identical to the one of Lemma
28
. To simplify notation, we write
K(\tau)=C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1)
and
f_{i}(\tau)=f_{Y|X}(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Proof of Case (i).
Let
\eta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
be an
\eta
-net with cardinality
\mathrm{card}(T_{\eta})\leq 1+1/\eta
. We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{v\in K(\tau^{\prime})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(\tau)\big(\tau^{\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime})\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}v\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{v\in K(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}(\tau)-f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})\big)\big(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}v\right|

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

(164)

Bound on
\mathbf{I}
.
By Lemma
15
and the union bound over
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\bar{f}\sqrt{t_{s,\eta,\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1}^{2}(t_{s,\eta,\delta})},

(165)

where
t_{s,\eta,\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+\log(1+1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
and
\pi_{n,1}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z/n
for
z\geq 0
.

Bound on
\mathbf{II}
.
We begin with deriving an upper bound on the supremum of an empirical processes indexed by an auxiliary function class. By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{W}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s}\right)^{s},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall u\in\mathcal{M}:\>\|u\|_{0}\leq s,\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau\in\mathcal{T}:\>K(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta)\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M});

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{W})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{2s_{\theta}}\right)^{2s_{\theta}},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall w\in\mathcal{W}:\>\|w\|_{0}\leq 2s_{\theta},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}:\>C^{p}(T_{\theta}(\tau)\cup T_{\theta}(\tau^{\prime}),0)\cap B^{p}(0,1)\subset 4\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{W}).

Let
\{g_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard normal random variables independent of
\{(X_{i},Y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\big\{f(X,Y,g)=g(X^{\prime}v)(X^{\prime}w)\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\big):\>v\in\mathcal{M},\>w\in\mathcal{W},\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}.

Note the following: First,
\mathcal{F}=\{hj:h\in\mathcal{H},\>j\in\mathcal{J}\}
, where
\mathcal{H}=\big\{h(X,Y)=\big(\tau-\mathbf{1}\{F_{Y|X}(Y|X)\leq\tau\}\big):\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\}
and
\mathcal{J}=\{j(X,g)=g(X^{\prime}v)(X^{\prime}w):v\in\mathcal{M},\>w\in\mathcal{W}\}
. The set
\mathcal{H}
is the difference of two VC-subgraph classes with VC-indices at most 2, respectively
(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.15 and Example 2.6.1)
. Thus,
\mathcal{H}
is VC-subgraph class with VC-index at most
3

(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.18)
. The function class
\mathcal{J}
is finite with
\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})=\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{W})
. By Assumption
1
and Lemma
36
, for
v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathcal{M}
,
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathcal{W}
arbitrary,

\displaystyle\left\|g^{2}(X^{\prime}v_{1})^{2}(X^{\prime}w_{1})^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g^{2}(X^{\prime}v_{1})^{2}(X^{\prime}w_{1})^{2}]-\big(g^{2}(X^{\prime}v_{2})^{2}(X^{\prime}w_{2})^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g^{2}(X^{\prime}v_{2})^{2}(X^{\prime}w_{2})^{2}]\big)\right\|_{P,\psi_{1/3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\left\|g^{2}(X^{\prime}v_{1})^{2}(X^{\prime}w_{1})^{2}-g^{2}(X^{\prime}v_{2})^{2}(X^{\prime}w_{2})^{2}\right\|_{P,\psi_{1/3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\sup_{u_{1},u_{2}}\left\|g^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\|(X^{\prime}u_{1})^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\|(X^{\prime}u_{2})^{2}\right\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\left(\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{2}+\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}(s)\varphi_{\max}(2s_{\theta})\left(\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{2}+\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}\right),

where the supremum in the third line is taken over all
u_{1},u_{2}
such that
\|u_{1}\|_{2},\|u_{2}\|_{2}\leq 1
and
\|u_{1}\|_{0}\leq s
and
\|u_{2}\|_{0}\leq 2s_{\theta}
. Therefore, for
j_{v},j_{u}\in\mathcal{J}
,

\displaystyle\|(j_{v}^{2}-Pj_{v}^{2})-(j_{u}^{2}-Pj_{u}^{2})\|_{P,\psi_{1}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}(s)\varphi_{\max}(2s_{\theta})\|v-u\|_{2}.

Thus, by Corollary
5
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\sqrt{t_{s,s_{\theta},\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1/3}^{2}(t_{s,s_{\theta},\delta})},

(166)

where
t_{s,s_{\theta},\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\delta)
and
\pi_{n,1/3}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z^{3}/n
for
z\geq 0
.

We now turn the bound on this process into a bound on
\mathbf{II}
. For any increasing and convex function
F
we have, for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
arbitrary,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{v\in K(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}(\tau)-f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})\big)\big(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}v\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sqrt{2\pi}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{v\in K(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\big(f_{i}(\tau)-f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})\big)\big(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}v\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(4\sqrt{2\pi}L_{f}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{\tau^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{v\in K(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime})\big)\big(\tau^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\}\big)X_{i}^{\prime}v\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(32(2+\vartheta)\sqrt{2\pi}L_{f}L_{\theta}\eta\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\right],

(167)

where
(a)
holds by Lemma 6.3 followed by Lemma 4.5 in
Ledoux and Talagrand, 1996
,
(b)
holds by Lemma
39
(note that the map
z\mapsto f_{Y|X}(z-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)-f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)
is Lipschitz-continuous and vanishes at 0), and
(c)
holds by Assumption
4
, Lemma
12
, and construction of
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{W}
.

Set
t_{s,s_{\theta},\eta,\delta}=s\log(5ep/s)+2s_{\theta}\log(5ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1+1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
. By Lemma
38
, eq. (
166
) and (
I.7
), and the union bound over
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}\lesssim L_{f}L_{\theta}(2+\vartheta)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\sqrt{t_{s,s_{\theta},\eta,\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1/3}^{2}(t_{s,s_{\theta},\eta,\delta})}\eta.

(168)

Conclusion.
Since
\eta\in(0,1)
is arbitrary, we can choose
\eta\asymp 1/(L_{f}L_{\theta}n)
.
Combine the bounds in eq. (
I.7
), (
166
), and (
168
), adjust the constants, and conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta)\bar{f}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\psi_{n}\big(t_{s,s_{\theta},n,\delta}\big),

where
t_{s,s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s\log(ep/s)+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(nL_{f}L_{\theta}/\delta)
and
\psi_{n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(1+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{z}+n^{-3/2}z^{3/2}\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

Proof of Case (ii).
The claim is an immediate consequence from the fact that the proof of case (i) uses an
\varepsilon
-net approximation of
s
-sparse sets. In fact, the proof of this case establish case (ii) first, and then uses Lemma
11
to deduce the case of
v\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
30
.

The proof is an easy modification of the proof of Lemma
29
. We only sketch the details:

Proof of Case (i).
To simplify notation, we write
K_{k}(\tau)=C^{p}(J_{k}(\tau),\vartheta_{k})\cap B^{p}(0,1)
for
k\in\{1,2\}
and
f_{i}(\tau)=f_{Y|X}(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Let
\eta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
be an
\eta
-net with cardinality
\mathrm{card}(T_{\eta})\leq 1+1/\eta
. We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sup_{s\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}^{2}(s)\big(s^{\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s^{\prime})\}\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sup_{s\in T_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau:|\tau-s|\leq\eta}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{k}\in K_{k}(s^{\prime})\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}^{2}(\tau)-f_{i}^{2}(s)\big)\big(s^{\prime}-\mathbf{1}\{Y_{i}\leq X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(s^{\prime})\}\big)(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{1})(X_{i}^{\prime}u_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

(169)

A trivial modification of Lemma
15
and the union bound over
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
yield, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}^{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\sqrt{t_{s_{1},s_{2},\eta,\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1/2}^{2}(t_{s_{1},s_{2},\eta,\delta})},

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},\eta,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+\log(1+1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
and
\pi_{n,1/2}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z^{2}/n
for
z\geq 0
.

By straightforward calculations as those leading up to eq. (
166
) we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\sqrt{t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1/4}^{2}(t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},\delta})},

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\delta)
and
\pi_{n,1/4}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z^{4}/n
for
z\geq 0
.

Note that
z\mapsto f_{Y|X}^{2}(z-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)-f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)
has Lipschitz constant
2L_{f}\bar{f}
and vanishes at 0. Hence, adapting the arguments in eq. (
I.7
) and eq. (
168
), we conclude that, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}\lesssim L_{f}L_{\theta}\bar{f}(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\sqrt{t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},\eta,\delta}}\sqrt{1+\pi_{n,1/4}^{2}(t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},\eta,\delta})}\eta,

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},\eta,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
.

Since
\eta\in(0,1)
is arbitrary, we can choose
\eta\asymp 1/(L_{f}L_{\theta}n)
.
Combine the bounds in on
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{II}
to conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{1})(2+\vartheta_{2})\bar{f}^{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{1})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{2})\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s_{\theta})\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\psi_{n}\big(t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},n,\delta}\big),

where
t_{s_{1},s_{2},s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s_{1}\log(ep/s_{1})+s_{2}\log(ep/s_{2})+s_{\theta}\log(ep/s_{\theta})+\log(nL_{f}L_{\theta}/\delta)
and
\psi_{n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(1+n^{-1/2}\sqrt{z}+n^{-3/2}z^{2}\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

Proof of Case (ii).
The claim is an immediate consequence from the fact that the proof of case (i) uses an
\varepsilon
-net approximation of
s
-sparse sets.
∎

Proof of Lemma
31
.

The proof strategy is similar to the one of Lemma
28
. However, instead of simply applying Corollary
4
, we us a combination of Lemmas
33
and
34
. This allows us to leverage the fact that
\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0}
for all
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
at the cost of additional
(\log n)
-factors.

Proof of Case (i).
To simplify notation, we write
K(\tau)=C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1)
and
f_{i}(\tau=f_{Y|X}(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X_{i})
fo
1\leq i\leq n
. By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{M}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{M})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s}\right)^{s},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall v\in\mathcal{M}:\>\|v\|_{0}\leq s,\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall\tau\in\mathcal{T}:\>K(\tau)\subset 2(2+\vartheta)\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{M}).

For
S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
,
v\in\mathcal{M}
, and
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
we define the following function classes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{S,v}

\displaystyle=\big\{h(X,Y)=\left(\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\big\}-\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}\right)X^{\prime}v,

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\mathrm{supp}(\theta)=S,\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0},\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}

\displaystyle=\big\{g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)h(X,Y):h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{S,v}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}.

Each
g\in\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}
and
h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}
is uniquely determined by the triplet
(v,\theta,\tau)
. We therefore also write
g_{v,\theta,\tau}
and
h_{v,\theta,\tau}
whenever we need to identify a function via its parameters. Let
\eta\in(0,1)
be arbitrary and
\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
be an
\eta
-net with cardinality
\mathrm{card}(T_{\eta})\leq 1+1/\eta
. We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}+\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})-f_{i}(\tau)\big)h(X_{i},Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[\big(f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})-f_{i}(\tau)\big)h(X_{i},Y_{i})\big]\right|

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

(170)

In the following we first derive an upper bound on
\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v}}
and then assemble a bound on
\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}
.

Bound on
\mathbf{I}
.
It is standard to verify that
\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}
is a VC-subgraph class with VC-index at most a constant multiple of
|S|+1
(see proof of Lemma
16
). Also,
G_{v}(X)=\bar{f}|X^{\prime}v|
is an envelope of
\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}
,
\|\max_{1\leq i\leq n}G_{v}(X_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim(\log n)\bar{f}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)
, and for all
g\in\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}
,

\displaystyle Pg^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{f}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[(X^{\prime}v)^{2}\big|F_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)-F_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta|X)\big|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{f}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[(X^{\prime}v)^{2}|X^{\prime}(\theta_{0}(\tau)-\theta)|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{0}\bar{f}^{3}\varphi_{\max}(s)\varphi_{\max}(|S|+s_{\theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{0}\varphi_{\max}(s)\big(1+\bar{f}^{3}\varphi_{\max}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big).

Thus, by Lemma
34
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{1}>0
(independent of
S,v,\tau
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}}\geq C_{1}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}}+C_{1}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\upsilon_{r_{0},n,2}(t)\right\}\leq e^{-t},

(171)

and by Lemma
33
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{2}>1
(independent of
S,v,\tau
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\upsilon_{r_{0},n,2}\big(|S|\log(1/r_{0})\big),

where
\upsilon_{r_{0},n,\gamma}(z)=\sqrt{r_{0}z}+\sqrt{\frac{\log^{\gamma}n}{n}z^{\gamma}}
for
z\geq 0
and
\gamma>0
. Set
t_{\eta,\delta}=\log(1+1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
. Now, eq. (
171
) and the union bound over
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
yield, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n,2}\big(|S|\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n,2}(t_{\eta,\delta})\big).

(172)

Bound on
\mathbf{II}
.
Let
\{g_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard normal random variables independent of
\{(X_{i},Y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
. By Lemma
11
there exist
\mathcal{W}\subset B^{p}(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{W})\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ep}{s_{\theta}}\right)^{s_{\theta}},\hskip 9.24994pt{}\forall w\in\mathcal{W}:\>\|w\|_{0}\leq s_{\theta},\hskip 9.24994pt{}B^{p}(0,1)\subset 4\mathrm{conv}(\mathcal{W}).

For
S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
,
v\in\mathcal{M}
, and
w\in\mathcal{W}
consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}=\big\{j(X,Y,g)=gh(X,Y)X^{\prime}w:h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}\big\}.

Again, we easily verify that
\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}
is a VC-subgraph class with VC-index at most a constant multiple of
|S|+2
(see proof of Lemma
16
). Also,
J_{v,w}(X,g)=|g||(X^{\prime}v)(X^{\prime}w)|
is an envelope of
\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}
,
\|\max_{1\leq i\leq n}J_{v,w}(X_{i},g_{i})\|_{\psi_{2/3}}\lesssim(\log n)^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})
, and for all
j\in\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}
,

\displaystyle Pj^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[g^{2}(X^{\prime}v)^{2}(X^{\prime}w)^{2}\big|F_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)-F_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta|X)\big|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{f}\mathbb{E}\left[g^{2}(X^{\prime}v)^{2}(X^{\prime}w)^{2}|X^{\prime}(\theta_{0}(\tau)-\theta)|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{0}\bar{f}\varphi_{\max}(s)\varphi_{\max}(2s_{\theta})\varphi_{\max}(|S|+s_{\theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{0}\varphi_{\max}(s)\varphi_{\max}(2s_{\theta})\big(1+\bar{f}\varphi_{\max}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big).

Thus, by Lemma
34
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{3}>0
(independent of
S,v,w
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}}\geq C_{3}\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}}+C_{3}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big(1+\bar{f}^{1/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\upsilon_{r_{0},n,3}(t)\right\}\leq e^{-t},

(173)

and by Lemma
33
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{4}>1
(independent of
S,v,w
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big(1+\bar{f}^{1/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\upsilon_{r_{0},n,3}\big(|S|\log(1/r_{0})\big),

where
\upsilon_{r_{0},n,\gamma}(z)=\sqrt{r_{0}z}+\sqrt{\frac{\log^{\gamma}n}{n}z^{\gamma}}
for
z\geq 0
and
\gamma>0
. Set
t_{s_{\theta},\delta}=2s_{\theta}\log(5ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1/\delta)
. (Note that the upper bound on
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v,\tau}}
is not tight, but it is a convenient choice since it matches with the other terms in eq. (
173
).) Now, by eq. (
173
) and the union bound over
w\in\mathcal{W}
we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in\mathcal{W}}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big(1+\bar{f}^{1/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n,3}\big(|S|\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n,3}(t_{s_{\theta},\delta})\big).

(174)

We now turn the bound on this process into a bound on
\mathbf{II}
. For any increasing and convex function
F
we have, for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
arbitrary,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})-f_{i}(\tau)\big)h(X_{i},Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[\big(f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})-f_{i}(\tau)\big)h(X_{i},Y_{i})\big]\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sqrt{2\pi}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\big(f_{i}(\tau^{\prime})-f_{i}(\tau)\big)h(X_{i},Y_{i})\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(4\sqrt{2\pi}L_{f}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}:|\tau^{\prime}-\tau|\leq\eta}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{S,v}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\big(X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau^{\prime})-X_{i}^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\big)h(X_{i},Y_{i})\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(16\sqrt{2\pi}L_{f}L_{\theta}\eta\sup_{w\in\mathcal{W}}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{J}_{S,v,w}}\right)\right],

(175)

where
(a)
holds by Lemma 6.3 followed by Lemma 4.5 in
Ledoux and Talagrand, 1996
,
(b)
holds by Lemma
39
(note that the map
z\mapsto f_{Y|X}(z-X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)-f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)
is Lipschitz-continuous and vanishes at 0), and
(c)
holds by Assumption
4
, Lemma
12
, and construction of
\mathcal{W}
.

Set
t_{s_{\theta},\eta,\delta}=2s_{\theta}\log(5ep/s_{\theta})+\log(1+1/\eta)+\log(1/\delta)
. By Lemma
38
, eq. (
174
) and (
I.7
), and the union bound over
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\eta}
we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}\lesssim L_{f}L_{\theta}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big(1+\bar{f}^{1/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n,3}\big(|S|\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n,3}(t_{s_{\theta},\eta,\delta}\big)\eta.

(176)

Conclusion.
Since
\eta\in(0,1)
is arbitrary, we can choose
\eta\asymp 1/(L_{f}L_{\theta}n^{1/2})
. Combining eq. (
I.7
), (
172
), and (
176
) yields, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v}}\lesssim\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\big(1+\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(|S|+s_{\theta})\big)\Big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n}\big(|S|\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(t_{s_{\theta},n,\delta})\Big),\end{split}

(177)

where
t_{s_{\theta},n,\delta}=2s_{\theta}\log(5ep/s_{\theta})+\log(L_{f}L_{\theta}n)+\log(1/\delta)
and
\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(z)=\sqrt{z}\big(\sqrt{r_{0}}+n^{-1/2}(\log n)\sqrt{z}+n^{-1}(\log n)^{3/2}z\big)
for
z\geq 0
.

Next, observe that
\mathrm{card}\big(\{S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}:|S|\leq k\}\big)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}{p\choose i}\leq\left(ep/k\right)^{k}
. Set
t_{s,k,s_{\theta},n,\delta}=s\log(5ep/s)+k\log(ep/k)+2s_{\theta}\log(5ep/s_{\theta})+\log(L_{f}L_{\theta}n)+\log(1/\delta)
. By eq. (
177
) and the union bound over
v\in\mathcal{M}
and
S\subset\{1,\ldots p\}
with
\mathrm{card}(S)\leq k
there exists an absolute constant
C_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{v\in\mathcal{M}}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\sup_{\mathrm{card}(S)\leq k}\frac{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v}}}{\Theta(s,k,s_{\theta})\big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n}\big(k\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(t_{s,k,s_{\theta},n,\delta})\big)}>C_{5}\right\}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{ep}{k}\right)^{k}e^{-k\log(ep/k)-\log n-\log(1/\delta)}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\delta,\end{split}

(178)

where
\Theta(s,k,s_{\theta})=\bar{f}^{3/2}\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(s)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(2s_{\theta})\big)\big(1+\varphi_{\max}^{1/2}(k+s_{\theta})\big)
. For
S\subset\{1,\ldots,p\}
define
\mathcal{G}_{S}=\left\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{S,v}:v\in C^{p}(J,\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1)\right\}
. By Lemma
12
and construction of
\mathcal{M}
, for all
S\subset\{1,\ldots,p\}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S}}\leq 2(2+\vartheta)\sup_{v\in\mathcal{M}}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}_{S,v}},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\bigcup_{S\subset\{1,\ldots p\},\>\mathrm{card}(S)\leq n}\mathcal{G}_{S}.

Hence, by eq. (
178
), with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\forall g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}:|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g_{v,\theta,\tau})|\lesssim 2(2+\vartheta)\Theta\big(s,\|\theta\|_{0},s_{\theta}\big)\big(\upsilon_{r_{0},n}\big(\|\theta\|_{0}\log(1/r_{0})\big)+\upsilon_{r_{0},n}(t_{s,\|\theta\|_{0},s_{\theta},n,\delta})\big).

Proof of Case (ii).
Observe that
s\mapsto s\log(ep/s)
and
s\mapsto\varphi_{\max}(s)
are monotone increasing on
[1,p]
. Thus, the bound of case (i) for
g_{v,\theta,\tau}\in\mathcal{G}
with
\|\theta\|_{0}=m
holds also for all
g_{v,\theta^{\prime},\tau}\in\mathcal{G}
with
\|\theta^{\prime}\|_{0}\leq m
. To conclude, adjust some constants.

Proof of Case (iii).
Note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{|\mathcal{G}|}\leq\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}^{A}}+\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}^{B}},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{A}

\displaystyle=\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\mathbf{1}\big\{X^{\prime}\theta<Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}|X^{\prime}u|,\>\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\|\theta\|_{0}\leq n,\>\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0},\>u\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{B}

\displaystyle\left\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)\mathbf{1}\big\{X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)<Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\}|X^{\prime}u|,\>\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\|\theta\|_{0}\leq n,\>\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0},\>u\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}(0,1),\>\tau\in\mathcal{T}\right\}.

Using the notation from case (i), we define, for
S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,p\}
,
u\in\mathcal{M}
, and
\tau\in\mathcal{T}
, the following function classes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{A}_{S,u}

\displaystyle=\big\{h(X,Y)=\mathbf{1}\big\{X^{\prime}\theta<Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}|X^{\prime}u|,

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\>\mathrm{supp}(\theta)=S,\|\theta-\theta_{0}(\tau)\|_{2}\leq r_{0},\tau\in\mathcal{T}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{A}_{S,u,\tau}

\displaystyle=\big\{g(X,Y)=f_{Y|X}^{2}(X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)|X)h(X,Y):h\in\mathcal{H}^{A}_{S,u}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{A}_{S,u}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{G}^{A}_{S,u,\tau},

and analogously
\mathcal{H}^{B}_{S,u},\mathcal{G}^{B}_{S,u,\tau},\mathcal{G}^{B}_{S,u}
.
The proof of the statement now follows by applying the same arguments as in the cases (i) and (ii) to these function classes. We only need to modify the argument that
\mathcal{H}^{A}_{S,u}
and
\mathcal{H}^{B}_{S,u}
are VC-subgraph classes of functions with VC-index at most a constant multiple of
|S|+2
:

The indicator
\mathbf{1}\big\{X^{\prime}\theta<Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}
can be written as the indicator of the set difference of
\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\}
and
\{Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)\}
. Both these sets are VC-classes of sets with VC-indices at most
|S|+3

(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Lemma 2.6.15)
and 2
(
Dudley, 2014
, Theorem 4.10 (a))
. By Lemma 2.6.17 (i) and (ii) in
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
the set difference of these VC-classes of sets is again a VC-class of sets with VC-index at most
|S|+3+2-1=|S|+4
. The same argument applies to the indicator
\mathbf{1}\big\{X^{\prime}\theta_{0}(\tau)<Y\leq X^{\prime}\theta\}
.

Proof of Case (iv).
This trivially follows from the fact that the proofs of cases (i) and (ii) rely on an
\varepsilon
-net approximation of
s
-sparse sets. In fact, the proofs of these cases establish case (iv) and then invoke Lemma
11
to deduce the case of
v\in C^{p}_{q}(J(\tau),\vartheta)\cap B^{p}_{2}(0,1)
. This completes the proof.
∎

I.8
Proofs of Section
H.2

Proof of Corollary
5
.

We first derive a bound on the symmetrized process
\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}
. Then, using Lemma
38
, we deduce a bound for the original process
\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}
. In the following, we slightly abuse notation and write
\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}
for
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sqrt{Pg^{2}}
. For
t,s>0
we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}>t+\sqrt{s}t\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\frac{\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}\left|\frac{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}-1\right|+\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\frac{\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}>t+\sqrt{s}t\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left\{\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}\frac{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}>t\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\frac{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}-1\right|>\sqrt{s}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}>\frac{t}{\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}^{2}-\|g\|_{P,2}^{2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}>s\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}^{2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II},

(179)

where the second inequality holds since for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
s,t>0
,

\displaystyle|ab|>st\hskip 9.24994pt{}\implies\hskip 9.24994pt{}|a|>s\hskip 9.24994pt{}\text{or}\hskip 9.24994pt{}|b|>t,

\displaystyle|a+b|>s+t\hskip 9.24994pt{}\implies\hskip 9.24994pt{}|a|>s\hskip 9.24994pt{}\text{or}\hskip 9.24994pt{}|b|>t,

and the third inequality holds since
|\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}|\leq\sqrt{|a-b|}
for all
a,b\geq 0
.

Bound on
\mathbf{I}
.
Define the following (data-dependent) classes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{g}=\left\{hg/\|g\|_{P_{n},2}:h\in\mathcal{H}\right\},\hskip 9.24994pt{}g\in\mathcal{G}.

For all
f\in\mathcal{F}_{g}
and any
e_{1},e_{2}\in\{-1,1\}
we have
|fe_{1}-fe_{2}|\leq 2|g|/\|g\|_{P_{n},2}
. Thus, conditionally on
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, the map
(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n})\mapsto\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{g}}
is a function of bounded differences with constant
c^{2}=4n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g^{2}(X_{i})/\|g\|_{P_{n},2}^{2}=4
. Hence, for all
u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{g}}\geq\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{g}}+\sqrt{u}\right\}\leq e^{-2u/c^{2}}=e^{-u/2}.

(180)

By construction of
\mathcal{F}_{g}
, the envelope
F_{g}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{g}}|f|
has
L_{2}(P_{n})
-semi-norm bounded by one and is VC subgraph with VC-index
V(\mathcal{H})
. Thus, by Dudley’s maximal inequality applied conditionally on
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and Theorem 2.6.7 in
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{g}}\lesssim\left\|F_{g}\right\|_{P_{n},2}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N\big(\varepsilon\left\|F_{g}\right\|_{P_{n},2},\mathcal{F}_{g},L_{2}(P_{n})\big)}d\varepsilon\lesssim\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})}.

(181)

Combine eq. (
180
) and (
181
) with the union bound over
g\in\mathcal{G}
to conclude that there exists an absolute constant
C_{1}>0
(independent of
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n},n,\mathcal{F}_{g},\mathcal{G}
) such that for all
u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}\left\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{g}}\geq C_{1}\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})}+\sqrt{u+\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})}\right\}\leq e^{-u/2}.

Clearly,
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}_{g}}\geq\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}/\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}
. Thus, in above display, take expectation with respect to
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and conclude that, for all
u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}}{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}}\geq C_{1}\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})}+\sqrt{\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})}+\sqrt{u}\right\}\leq e^{-u/2}.

(182)

Bound on
\mathbf{II}
.
Recall that
\mathcal{G}^{2}=\{g^{2}:g\in\mathcal{G}\}
. Hence,
\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}^{2}}=\sqrt{n}\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}^{2}-\|g\|_{P,2}^{2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}
. Therefore, by eq. (
44
) and Corollary
4
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{2}>0
such that, for all
u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\big\|\|g\|_{P_{n},2}^{2}-\|g\|_{P,2}^{2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}>C_{2}K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}\big(\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})\big)+C_{2}K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(u)\right\}\leq e^{-u},

(183)

where
\delta=\sup_{g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{G}^{2}}\rho(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(z)=\sqrt{z/n}+z^{1/\alpha}/n
for
z\geq 0
.

Conclusion.
Set

\displaystyle t=\left(\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})}+\sqrt{\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})}+\sqrt{u}\right)\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}\hskip 9.24994pt{}\mathrm{and}\hskip 9.24994pt{}s=\left(K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}\big(\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})\big)+K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(u)\right)\big\|\|g\|_{P,2}\big\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{-2}.

Now, combine eq. (
I.8
), (
182
), and (
183
) and conclude that there exists an absolute constant
C_{3}>0
such that, for all
u>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}>C_{3}\left(\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})}+\sqrt{\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})}+\sqrt{u}\right)\left(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}\big(\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})\big)}+\sqrt{K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(u)}\right)\right\}\\
\leq 2e^{-u}e^{-u}.\end{split}

(184)

Since for any convex and increasing function
F:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(2\|\mathbb{G}_{n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right)\right],

Lemma
38
and eq. (
184
) imply that, there exist cosntants
c,c^{\prime}\geq 0
(depending on
\alpha
) such that, with probability at least
1-ce^{-c^{\prime}t}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}\lesssim\left(\sqrt{V(\mathcal{H})}+\sqrt{\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})}+\sqrt{t}\right)\left(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}\big(\log\mathrm{card}(\mathcal{G})\big)}+\sqrt{K\delta\pi_{n,\alpha}^{2}(t)}\right).

To conclude, adjust some absolute constants.
∎

Proof of Lemma
36
.

Recall Young’s inequality:
\prod_{i=1}^{K}x_{i}^{\lambda_{i}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K}\lambda_{i}x_{i}
for all
x_{i},\lambda_{i}\geq 0
,
i=1,\ldots,K
with
\sum_{i=1}^{K}\lambda_{i}=1
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\|X_{i}\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}=1
for all
i=1,\ldots,K
. Thus, the claim of the lemma follows if we can show the following: If
\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(|X_{i}|^{\alpha})\right]\leq 2
for all
i=1,\ldots,K
, then
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\prod_{i=1}^{K}|X_{i}|^{\alpha/K})]\leq 2
. This assertion follows from straightforward calculations:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\psi_{\alpha/K}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{K}X_{i}\right)\right]+1=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\prod_{i=1}^{K}|X_{i}|^{\alpha/K}\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i=1}^{K}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{}=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{K}\exp\left(\frac{1}{K}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{K}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\right)\right]\right)\leq 2,

where in the first and second inequalities we have used Young’s inequality.
∎

Proof of Lemma
37
.

Let
t\geq 0
be arbitrary. Set
A:=\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\xi(\omega)>\phi(t)\right\}
. By the premise,
\phi(t)\leq\int_{A}\xi d\mathbb{P}\leq\phi\left(\varphi^{-1}\left(1/\mathbb{P}\{A\}\right)\right)
. Thus,
\phi(t)\leq\phi\left(\varphi^{-1}\left(1/\mathbb{P}\{A\}\right)\right)
. Solving for
\mathbb{P}\{A\}
yields the claim.
∎

Proof of Lemma
38
.

We split the proof into two cases with similar yet distinct proofs.

Case
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Denote by
\varphi^{-1}
the inverse of
\varphi
and note that
\varphi^{-1}
is convex and increasing. Therefore,
F(z)=\max\{\varphi^{-1}(z)-t,0\}
is also convex and increasing. Hence, by assumption, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\varphi^{-1}(X)\geq s\right\}ds=\mathbb{E}[F(X)]\leq\mathbb{E}[F(Y)]=\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\varphi^{-1}(Y)\geq s\right\}ds\\
&\leq\int_{t}^{\infty}c_{1}e^{-c_{2}s^{\alpha}}ds=\frac{c_{1}}{\alpha c_{2}^{1/\alpha}}\int_{c_{2}t^{\alpha}}^{\infty}u^{1/\alpha-1}e^{-u}du=\frac{c_{1}}{\alpha c_{2}^{1/\alpha}}\Gamma\left(1/\alpha,c_{2}t^{\alpha}\right),\end{split}

(185)

where
\Gamma(a,z)=\int_{z}^{\infty}u^{a-1}e^{-u}du
is the incomplete Gamma function.

By Theorem 1.1 and Proposition 2.10 in
Pinelis, 2020
, for all
z\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Gamma(a,z)\leq\begin{cases}z^{a-1}e^{-x}+(a-1)G_{a-1}(z)&1<a<2\\
G_{a}(z)&a\geq 2,\end{cases}

(186)

where
G_{a}(z)=\frac{(z+b_{a})^{a}-z^{a}}{ab_{a}}e^{-z}
and
b_{a}=\Gamma(a+1)^{1/(a-1)}
. Thus, by eq. (
186
) there exist constants
c,c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
(depending on
a>1
only) such that, for all
z\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Gamma(a,z)\leq(cz^{a}+c^{\prime}z^{a-1}+c^{\prime\prime})e^{-z}\leq c^{\prime\prime\prime}e^{-z/a}.

(187)

Combine eq. (
187
) and (
185
) to conclude that there exists a constant
c_{3}>1
(depending on
\alpha,c_{1}
only) such that for all
t\geq 0
and all
u\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\varphi^{-1}(X)\geq t\right\}\leq\frac{1}{u}\int_{t-u}^{t}\mathbb{P}\left\{\varphi^{-1}(X)\geq s\right\}ds\leq\frac{c_{3}e^{\alpha c_{2}u^{\alpha}}}{c_{2}^{1/\alpha}u}e^{-\alpha c_{2}t^{\alpha}}.

Optimizing over
u
yields
u^{*}=(1/\alpha^{2}c_{2})^{1/\alpha}
and, hence, for all
t\geq(1/\alpha^{2}c_{2})^{1/\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\varphi^{-1}(X)\geq t\right\}\leq c_{3}e^{1/\alpha}e^{-\alpha c_{2}t^{\alpha}}.

Since
c_{3}e^{1/\alpha}e^{-\alpha c_{2}t^{\alpha}}\geq 1
for
t\leq(1/c_{2})^{1/\alpha}
, this bound holds also for all
0\leq t<(1/c_{2})^{1/\alpha}
.

Case
\alpha\in[1,\infty)
.
The proof strategy is the same as for case (i), but the calculations are simpler. Denote by
\varphi^{-1}
the inverse of
\varphi
and note that
z\mapsto\left(\varphi^{-1}(z)\right)^{\alpha}
is convex and increasing. Therefore,
F(z)=\max\left\{\left(\varphi^{-1}(z)\right)^{\alpha}-t,0\right\}
is also convex and increasing. We have, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\varphi^{-1}(X)\right)^{\alpha}\geq s\right\}ds=\mathbb{E}[F(X)]\leq\mathbb{E}[F(Y)]=\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\varphi^{-1}(Y)\right)^{\alpha}\geq s\right\}ds\leq\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{-c_{2}t}.

Thus, we have, for all
t\geq 0
and all
u\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\varphi^{-1}(X)\right)^{\alpha}\geq t\right\}\leq\frac{1}{u}\int_{t-u}^{t}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\varphi^{-1}(X)\right)^{\alpha}\geq s\right\}ds\leq\frac{c_{1}e^{c_{2}u}}{c_{2}u}e^{-c_{2}t}.

Optimizing over
u
yields, for all
t\geq 1/c_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left(\varphi^{-1}(X)\right)^{\alpha}\geq t\right\}\leq c_{1}e^{1-c_{2}t}.

Again, since
c_{1}e^{1-c_{2}t}\geq 1
for
t\leq 1/c_{2}
, this bound holds also for all
0\leq t<1/c_{2}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
39
.

The proof is a straightforward modification of the classical proof of Corollary 3.17 in
Ledoux and Talagrand, 1996
. Let
\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}
be arbitrary. We have the following:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\frac{1}{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\varphi_{i}\big(f(X_{i})\big)h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\Big(\varphi_{i}\big(f(X_{i})\big)-\varphi_{i}\big(\tilde{f}(X_{i})\big)\Big)h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\varphi_{i}\big(\tilde{f}(X_{i})\big)h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\Big(\varphi_{i}\big(f(X_{i})\big)-\varphi_{i}\big(f^{\prime}(X_{i})\big)\Big)h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right],

where (a) holds by convexity of
F
and (b) holds since
\varphi_{i}(0)=0
and symmetry of
g_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq n
. The expression in above display can be further upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(\sup_{f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\Big(f(X_{i})-f^{\prime}(X_{i})\Big)h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right],

(188)

since for all
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
and
h\in\mathcal{H}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\varphi_{i}\big(f(X_{i})\big)-\varphi_{i}\big(f^{\prime}(X_{i})\big)\Big)\Big)^{2}h^{2}(X_{i})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(f(X_{i})-f^{\prime}(X_{i})\Big)^{2}h^{2}(X_{i}),

and hence the the Gaussian contraction theorem
(e.g.
Ledoux and Talagrand, 1996
, Theorem 3.15)
applies. To conclude the proof, upper bound eq. (
188
) by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[F\left(2\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}f(X_{i})h(X_{i})\right|\right)\right].

∎
